| 2026-08-02 | resolved | Developer workflow — test-suite runtime | **The hypothesis profile docstring still promised a 5-minute suite from when it had 1200 tests.** `tests/conftest_hypothesis.py` documented `ci-fast` as targeting "< 5 minutes wall-clock" and `ci-deep` as "~10 min"; both dated from a ~1200-test suite that is now ~4400, so both had been wrong for months. It also named the wrong invocation — `pytest tests/ -q --hypothesis-profile=ci-deep` predates `run_tests.sh`'s tiering and would run the suite serially without the `--full` marker composition. Replaced with measured, dated wall-clock: `./run_tests.sh` per-commit ci-fast **206 s** (2026-08-01); `./run_tests.sh --full` ci-fast **485 s** (2026-08-02); `./run_tests.sh --full` ci-deep **25m 52s** (2026-08-02). The load-bearing number is the ratio: ci-deep is **~3.2x** the ci-fast `--full` run, not the ~20x the 50→1000 `max_examples` ratio implies, because only the hypothesis properties scale and they are a minority of the suite's cost — exactly what the old "5 min vs 10 min" pair got wrong in both directions, implying a 2x cost for a 20x example count. The ci-deep figure is not bespoke: it is the 2026-08-02 nightly (`temp/reports/nightly/`, GREEN, 4415/4418), which re-measures it daily for free. Verification: `pytest tests/ -q --collect-only` (run 2026-08-02) **4418 collected** — the module still imports and its `settings.register_profile` side effects still run, the only thing a docstring edit here could plausibly break; `pytest tests/sim/test_ballistics.py -q --hypothesis-profile=ci-deep --collect-only` (run 2026-08-02) **15 collected**, i.e. the profile name still resolves — an unregistered profile is a hard error at startup. | [2026-08-02-hypothesis-profile-timings](2026-08-02-hypothesis-profile-timings.md) |

| 2026-08-01 | resolved | Developer workflow — test-suite hygiene | **Nine tests computed a verdict and returned it — pytest discards a test's return value, so they could not fail.** 51 tests across `test_motor_guard.py` and `test_kinematics.py` ended in `return passed` / `return True`; for **nine** of them, which had no other enforcement, the computed verdict went nowhere. The other 42 do enforce — 33 via plain `assert` statements, 9 via `np.testing.assert_*` calls only, so their returns were only noise (and a hard error under pytest 8). The nine include **every test in `test_kinematics.py` bar the component-cross regression test** — the IK/FK round-trips, the analytical Jacobian against finite differences, and the `Jdot @ twist` bias term. Root cause: both files began as standalone scripts whose `main()` aggregates `results.append(test_fn())`, and when pytest later adopted them by filename the pass/fail contract was never converted from "return a bool" to "assert". **Two tests gained checks their originals lacked**: `test_loop_timing` now asserts `n_cycles > 0` *first* — with zero cycles the jitter statistic is vacuously small, so a loop that never ticked would have *satisfied* the gate — and `test_singularity_map`, previously `return True  # informational only`, now asserts the invariant it was already computing and printing (zero poses with Jacobian condition number > 100). `main()` becomes `test_fn(); passed = True`, which is load-bearing: a pure-assert conversion makes a *passing* test return `None`, and `all([None, ...])` is `False`, so script mode would have reported total failure. The 42 bare returns were stripped by an AST pass restricted to each test function's own top-level returns, leaving returns inside nested helper closures alone. Carries the refactor programme's deferred motor_guard `nightly` demotion (3 tests), which empties the default gate's serial phase. **The first classification said 18 vacuous tests and was wrong**, and the way it was wrong is the lesson: it looked for `ast.Assert` nodes and missed `np.testing.assert_allclose(...)`, which is a *function call*, so the 9 tests enforced *only* by an `assert_*` call scored as unprotected, inflating the count from 9 to 18 — reading the accused bodies caught it, and re-running with "any construct that can raise" gave the real 9. A structural query answers the question you encoded, not the question you asked. Verification: `pytest tests/motion/test_kinematics.py tests/motion/test_motor_guard.py -q`, run 2026-08-01: **57 passed**, zero `ReturnNotNone` warnings; script mode still aggregates correctly (**ALL 45 TESTS PASSED** / **ALL 6 TESTS PASSED**, both exit 0); `--collect-only -m "serial and not nightly"` collects **0 of 4418** while `-m serial` still collects **9**. | [2026-08-01-vacuous-tests-return-not-assert](2026-08-01-vacuous-tests-return-not-assert.md) |
| 2026-08-01 | resolved | Refactor programme Phase 4 — teensy_link to the repo root | **`git mv controller/teensy_link teensy_link`; 327 refs across 134 files, 89 files rewritten.** Owner-decided destination: the repo root keeps the bridge running the LIVE tree (both launch files already inject the repo root on `PYTHONPATH`), so a wire-format edit is live at the next relaunch instead of sitting behind a `colcon build` whose omission is silent. Two things the mechanical rewrite would have missed: `protocol.py` derived the repo root as `../..` from its own file (now `..`, and it has a `sys.path` fallback so the break would have surfaced late, not loudly), and the bridge **stopped importing CasADi** — `from controller.teensy_link import …` executed `controller/__init__.py` → `mpc`, and the ROS package now has zero `controller.*` imports. The compat shim `controller/teensy_link.py` is `sys.modules` **aliasing**, never a re-export, and aliases every submodule explicitly: two identities for `RpcError`/`RpcTimeout` would let a `raise` sail through an `except` written against the other path, silently. TEMPORARY — delete after 2026-09. The move also **claims the top-level name `teensy_link`, which `tests/teensy_link/` already claims** — at `sys.path = [tests, repo]` the import resolves to the test directory (reproduced live), and it is green today only by pytest's prepend-mode insertion order; now pinned in `test_compat_shim.py` (the named diagnosis in front of an error that otherwise points at a submodule), the same guard `test_sim_import_style.py` installs for `sim`, and `teensy_link` joined that file's `_SCAN_DIRS` so relocating a package out of `controller/` cannot silently drop it out of the bare-import scan. Wire bytes unmoved (xlang digest `1383b3fc18dc085c51eba58979ef60a3898b2ff74dc99931926d04c0bc7ceccb` identical before and after). Gate `./run_tests.sh --full` (2026-08-01): **parallel 4406 passed, 3 xfailed in 448.43 s; serial 9 passed, 4409 deselected in 40.52 s; total 494 s — RESULT: PASS** (exit 0). Hardware smoke **CONFIRMED 2026-08-01 23:17** (operator rebuilt, relaunched, ran LEVELLING end-to-end; log reviewed clean — see the entry's CONFIRMED note). | [2026-08-01-teensy-link-repo-root](2026-08-01-teensy-link-repo-root.md) |
| 2026-08-01 | resolved | Refactor programme Phase 6 (slice 2) — structure cleanups | **Four structural cleanups in the plan’s mandated order; no control-path behaviour changed.** **(1) One import root for sim/**: 179 bare-style imports across 71 files became `sim.*`, and ~30 hand-rolled `sys.path` blocks became one `sim/_paths.bootstrap_paths()` used only by runnable entry scripts — library modules under `sim/` now never touch `sys.path`. The bug closed is *two module objects for one file*, which makes a `patch()` against one identity silently patch nothing. `tests/sim/test_sim_import_style.py` is the enforcement point (AST grep, fails on any new bare import or hand-rolled path block). **Deliberate deviation**: the bootstrap installs three roots, not the plan’s four — `sim/` is retired, because keeping it keeps the dual-identity mechanism live in every bootstrapped process while providing nothing after the conversion (verified live: with `sim/` installed, a bare import still mints a twin, so “four roots” and “one module object” cannot both hold). **(2) `controller/demo/` → `sim/juggle_planner/`** (49 refs, 21 files): every consumer is a sim script/test/probe, while `controller/` is imported by the hardware MPC loop; the move dissolved the boundary that forced seven hand-copied hand-stroke constants into `juggle_optimizer.py`, now real imports from `sim.hand.*`, printed bit-identical before/after. **(3) Clock-offset dedup**: the character-identical ROS-clock→`perf_counter` estimator in `trajectory_node` and `catch_coordinator_node` is now `jugglebot/clock_offset.py` (pure, clock injected); `tests/ros/test_clock_offset.py` drives new code and a verbatim transcription of the old through one scripted clock and asserts **exact float equality**, 30 refreshes past the 20-deep trim. `reload_coordinator_node`’s single-read variant is left alone, with a comment saying the reconciliation is an open decision. **(4) Bridge test harness**: 21 files imported `_build_paired_node` from a *test module* and `_teardown` was copy-pasted 19 times → non-collected `tests/ros/_bridge_harness.py`. Collect-only gates: 4398 → 4402 (+4 guard tests) → 4402 (move) → 4410 (+8 clock tests) → 4410 (harness extraction adds/removes none). **Five real breaks that the green suite passed over** were found by review and fixed before the commit, all one shape — *code the suite never executes*: `controller/generate_solver.py` (the AOT solver compile, the one documented hardware-path build command) lost the roots `mujoco_plant` used to self-install; `sim/viz/reference_plot.py`'s lazy `motion.geometry` import survived the drop of its wrong fifth root; and three probes plus `tools/motion_onset_cogging_study.py` carried module-level renames leaving dangling `_REPO_ROOT`/`_REPO`/`_root` uses inside `main()` — three of them in the *result-writing* path, reached only after the whole sweep runs. An AST undefined-global-name scan over the 135 `.py` files in this slice's three commits now returns **0 hits**; `--help` exits 0 for the four CLIs; `generate_solver`'s import chain is checked **by import, never by running it** (it unlinks `mpc_gen.so` first). Also fixed: `REFRESH_PERIOD_S` was documented as the nodes' timer period while both nodes still hardcoded `30.0` — a shared constant with **no reader**, the same duplicated-timing-constant shape the unit set out to kill, now wired into both `create_timer` sites and AST-pinned against reverting to a literal; 19 entry scripts named the retired `sim/` root in their bootstrap comment; `juggle_planner` joined `_SIM_TOP_LEVEL` (the newest package was the one hole the dual-identity class could re-open through); and `sim.__file__` is now pinned, because `tests/sim/__init__.py` makes tests/sim a regular package *also named* `sim`. `./run_tests.sh --full`, run 2026-08-01 after the fixes: **parallel 4398 passed, 3 xfailed in 444.94 s; serial 9 passed, 4401 deselected in 40.23 s; total 491 s — RESULT: PASS** (exit 0). | [2026-08-01-structure-cleanups](2026-08-01-structure-cleanups.md) |
| 2026-08-01 | resolved | Refactor programme Phase 6 (slice 1) — analysis safety nets | **Four nets over things that rot without anything going red; no control-path code.** `logbook_search` stopped dropping malformed entries in silence — `scan_entries()` returns `(entries, warnings)`, a titled-but-incomplete entry still loads (it is real prior art) but warns, and `tests/sim/test_logbook_front_matter.py` fails if any committed entry would warn; all 180 entry files already passed. The contract got its normative half too: `logbook/README.md` now names title/type/date/status as REQUIRED with an ISO `date`, and warns that the no-dependency parser keeps a trailing `# comment` verbatim. New `tools/gen_choreography_map.py` renders `ros_ws/docs/choreography.md` — **75 distinct wires across 140 endpoints** — by pure `ast`: names resolve only through exact paths (literal, module/class constant, ROS-parameter default tagged `param:<key>`) and anything else prints `UNRESOLVED(<expr>)`, because a map that guesses is worse than none. Review found the resolver breaking its own rule in **three latent places**, all closed here: `self.X` was answered from a same-named *module* constant (the `ast.Attribute` path is deleted outright — keying constants by bare name cannot tell an instance attribute from a module constant, and no endpoint uses the shape), a nested-`def` binding leaked upward into the enclosing function's scope with provenance `literal`, and the doc's closing line claimed "every name resolved to a literal" while the body tagged one as `param:`. The drift test could not have caught any of them — it pins doc == generator, not generator == source truth. The map also presented Phase 3's two parked nodes (`motion_bridge_node`, `mpc_bridge_node`) as live consumers across 8 wires; they now render `(not launched)`, cross-checked against `jugglebot_launch.py` **in both directions** by a test. `stewart-fk.js` (the repo's only duplicate kinematics, previously pinned by a manual browser page) now has 25 Python-generated golden poses spanning 11.6–246 mm of the 280 mm stroke, replayed through the real JS under node with **no shim**: IK agrees to 1.1e-13 mm, FK pose to 4.6e-7 mm, a mutated `revs * MM_TO_REV` moves the answer **261.9 mm** against a 5e-6 mm gate, and `FK_POS_TOL_MM` is now *derived* — sweeping the JS solver's residual tolerance shows pose error is bounded by ~0.53x it, so the gate is 10x the algorithmic ceiling, not 10x a lucky sample. Plus the stale-doc sweep, whose every fix the entry enumerates: `git log --diff-filter=D` proved all nine "missing" `tools/archived/` harnesses were deleted deliberately in 67889a6, which closed that rot pocket instead of deferring it. `ros_ws/docs/safety.md` §3 (a step limit in the deleted `can_node.py`; the clamp lives on in `controller/teensy_link/setpoint_pump.py`) is named in Phase 3's existing STALE banner rather than spot-patched — the file is owed one re-framing pass. | [2026-08-01-analysis-safety-nets](2026-08-01-analysis-safety-nets.md) |
| 2026-08-01 | resolved | Refactor programme Phase 5 — config freshness contract | **Production stays build-frozen; the confusion dies by observability instead.** `generate_config.py --check` renders all 16 planned artifacts (5 generated + 11 delivered copies) in memory and diffs them against disk — check and write consume the SAME `build_artifacts()` plan, so an artifact cannot be added to one path and forgotten in the other, and check mode performs **no writes at all** (`mtime_ns` + size + sha256 identical on 16/16 across a run, pinned as a test). Three deliberate properties, each closing a way the gate could have become useless: the **exit code covers the 14 in-repo destinations only** (the other two live in `../BallButler`, a separate repo on its own branch — letting it fail this repo's pre-commit gate, or raise this repo's bring-up banner over two Teensy headers no node here reads, would spend the banner's only asset; its drift still prints as `EXTERNAL DRIFT:`); **anything not checked is announced** (`SKIPPED:` / `NOT CHECKED:` — with the hardware YAML absent, 9 of 16 artifacts silently dropped out behind a green `CONFIG FRESH: 7`, exactly the false-green the design forbids); and it **compares text, not bytes** (the write path's `newline=None` emits CRLF on Windows, where a byte compare would report permanent 16/16 drift on a clean checkout — and this repo is run from a Win10 clone). `jugglebot_launch.py` reports two links separately because the fixes differ — (A) source YAML → repo artifacts via `--check`, (B) repo artifacts → **installed** copies via byte compare off `AMENT_PREFIX_PATH`, the link `--check` structurally cannot see and the one that actually bites; drift prints a `!`-bordered banner naming both commands + RELAUNCH, and a check that could not RUN — including a module missing from either side of link B — reports `PARTIAL` rather than a green OK. It **never blocks**: 10 s timeout (30× the measured 0.33 s; `ros2 launch` prints nothing until the description returns, so the bound is the length of an unexplained frozen terminal), every failure path degrades to one line. The repo root is walked up from the launch file itself so a `git worktree` build compares against its own tree. `teensy_bridge_node` logs the resolved path + sha256 + mtime (`%z` offset, so it is reconcilable across a DST change) of the `hardware_config` module it **actually imported** (off `hw.__file__`) once at boot. **Phase 5 is partially delivered**: plan item 1 asked every node to log its effective tuning values + a config hash — delivered is one node, identity only; item 3 (`friction_ff_params.py` resolution order) is untouched pending the owner decision. Honest scope, per the plan: this would NOT have caught `24608bb`'s stale binary — that is Phase 7's pio-clean forcing. | [2026-08-01-config-drift-gate](2026-08-01-config-drift-gate.md) |
| 2026-08-01 | resolved | Refactor programme Phase 7a — ERR_TIMEOUT recount | **The hand-ack epidemic survived the bus-role swap at the same rate, on a bus with literally zero recorded wire errors — it is not the CAN3 drive-path fault.** The RPC code is recoverable offline from `~/.ros/log/*/launch.log`, **not** from the bags (no recorded topic carries an RPC status; `/rosout` is not in the record list) — the bags supply the context and the date. Pre-swap pooled (15 sessions, 07-23→07-31 13:56): prime **59/125 = 47.2 %**, arm **135/258 = 52.3 %**; post-swap (07-31 14:51, the only post-swap session with hand dispatches): prime **4/8**, arm **4/8**. **Every recoverable HAND_TRAJ_CMD failure is `ERR_TIMEOUT`, none `ERR_BUS_DOWN`** (distinct firmware paths: `hand_ops.cpp:34` gate vs `:42/:45/:56` sends), and every one is a Teensy-returned code — `rpc.py:86` synthesises host-side `RpcTimeout` with the same `METHOD: ERR_TIMEOUT` prefix, but `grep "ERR_TIMEOUT after"` finds it only on HOME/STATE_READ/STATE_WRITE/GET_AXIS_VERSIONS, never on the hand path. In the four codes-visible sessions `prime_fail + arm_fail == ERR_TIMEOUT lines` **exactly**, 93/93, validating the derived arm counter (needed because `20d01e9`, 07-24, demoted the arm-ack failure WARN→DEBUG). **Decisive cell**: post-swap bag `2026-07-31_14-51-38` (n=10513) reads `bus1/2/3_health` OK 100 %, `fault_state` NONE, `can3_errors` `tecInc=0 … bit1=0 gated=0` on **every** sample — zero wire errors, zero TX-gate refusals, still 4/8 acks failing ⇒ every failure was `bus.write() <= 0`, a FlexCAN write rejection on an error-free bus. **The epidemic era has no comparable cell and never will**: `can3_errors` is absent from the 07-27 and 07-29 bags entirely (33 keys) and reads `unknown (never seen)` in the pre-swap 07-31 13:56 bag, so the pre-swap half rests on the coarse 10 Hz health classifier alone — a counter-level pre/post comparison is impossible retroactively. **Open tension, deliberately left open**: a write rejection means the trajectory frame was never enqueued, but `catch_coordinator_node.py:159-166` encodes "the ack lies — frames were observed transmitted after a failed ack" as the reason `_MAX_ARM_DISPATCHES` KEEPS the latch; both cannot be true, the `tx_write_fail` counter settles it, and **the latch behaviour stays as it is until then**. A tempting `mpc_active` correlation (p≈9e-4) killed as pure selection. Swap dated 14:17–14:51 on 07-31 from the `bus3_health` marker row (keys 36→37), not the 15:29 commit stamp. Needed to close: `tx_write_fail` split from `tx_gated`, per-send attribution, a TX-queue high-water mark, a `link_status` hand-ack-failure counter, and one ordinary post-swap sitting (the CI on 4/8 is enormous). Read-only throughout; closes open item 4 of the 07-31 CAN3 entry, refuting its hypothesis. | [2026-08-01-err-timeout-recount](2026-08-01-err-timeout-recount.md) |

Docs re-framed: the **Teensy-side `MAX_DEVIATION` guard** is the leg-path safety authority, not `motor_guard`. Collect-only equality gate recorded: 3890 + 3 + 432 = 4325 = 4316 + 9, all 9 serial tests accounted for. `test_retime.py` bisection 60 → 25 iterations, verified by a 54-case corpus diff with **zero** verdict changes. | [2026-08-01-nightly-tier-and-mpc-dormancy](2026-08-01-nightly-tier-and-mpc-dormancy.md) |

| 2026-07-31 | resolved | Developer workflow — test-suite hygiene | **The 4→5 protocol bump was done correctly and still left the branch red: `PROTOCOL_VERSION` was pinned in two places and only one of them is named in the procedure that maintains it.** `tests/teensy_link/test_protocol_codec.py::test_constants_match_firmware_spec` failed `assert 5 == 4` after `bf1e9a5` ("cone PROFILE slot, UDP v5, FW8") — a bump that had updated every artefact the checklist names: the generator spec, the generated Python module, the committed C++ header, and the canonical pin. That canonical pin, `tests/firmware/test_udp_protocol_xlang.py::test_protocol_version_frozen`, is the designed tripwire: it pins the spec with a full bump-history comment, cross-checks spec == generated Python == committed C++ header, and prints the fleet-reflash procedure on failure. The second pin was a bare literal in `test_protocol_codec.py` that is **not** named in that procedure, so following the checklist to the letter still ships a red suite. **The fix deletes the duplicate rather than bumping it** — a 4→5 edit is the thirty-second fix the failure message invites, and it rebuilds the trap for the next bump. Pin 2 was fully redundant (`p.PROTOCOL_VERSION` is imported from the generated module pin 1 already checks), so it carried no independent signal and full maintenance cost; the assertions pin 1 does *not* cover are kept — `PORT_STREAM`, `PORT_RPC`, `NUM_LEGS`, `NUM_AXES`, `HEARTBEAT_HZ`. Also de-stamped a stale `PROTOCOL_VERSION 4` comment at `teensy_bridge_node.py:216`. Class of failure: **one fact pinned in two places with only one of them named in the procedure that maintains it** — a duplicated pin is not twice the protection, it is one real check plus one time bomb. Verification: scoped `pytest tests/teensy_link/ tests/firmware/test_udp_protocol_xlang.py -q`, run 2026-07-31: **231 passed**; full gate `./run_tests.sh`, run 2026-07-31: **parallel 446 s (4287 passed, 3 xfailed) \| serial 32 s (7 passed) \| total 478 s** — `RESULT: PASS`. | [2026-07-31-protocol-version-duplicate-pin](2026-07-31-protocol-version-duplicate-pin.md) |

| 2026-07-31 | resolved | Developer workflow — test-suite runtime | **The pre-commit gate was 27:40 and had quietly stopped being run — 4 xdist workers on `--dist loadfile` cut it to ~8 minutes, and the ROS plugin suppression turned out never to have worked.** Serial baseline (`pytest tests/ -q`, run 2026-07-31 on the Jetson, 6 cores / 7.2 GB, isolated worktree at `a7da5c5`): **4297 tests / 1660.65 s (27:40)**. The suite now runs as a parallel phase (`-n 4 --dist loadfile`) plus a 7-test serial phase through `./run_tests.sh` in **~8 min — a 3.5× cut, no test rewritten and no coverage dropped** (bare `pytest tests/ -q` stays serial, unmodified and still equivalent, so the parallel setup is never load-bearing for correctness). **`--dist loadfile` is the load-bearing choice**: keeping every test in a file on a single worker is what made this adoptable without auditing 4300 tests individually — the 49 module-/session-scoped fixtures build once per file exactly as they do serially, and the two files that bind **fixed** localhost ports (`test_motor_guard.py` :15555/:15556, `test_teensy_bridge_node_setpoint.py` :5599) cannot collide; the default `--dist load` scatters a file across workers and both hazards return nondeterministically. **Second, unrelated defect found and fixed**: the `-p no:launch_testing` / `-p no:launch_testing_ros` entries in `pyproject.toml` had **never disabled anything** — those are module names and pytest's `-p no:` takes pytest11 *entry-point* names (`launch`, `launch_ros`), and a `-p no:` that matches no plugin is a silent no-op, so both ROS launch plugins had been active on every run since the addopts block was written. The consequence is real: the launch plugin imports every collected module at collection time, so one unimportable test file aborts all 4297 tests as a single collection error — exactly how this investigation's first benchmark attempt died. **Worker count pinned at 4, not `-n auto`** (which selects 6 here), from a measured 2×2 of workers × BLAS thread pinning, all 2026-07-31: (default, 4) 444.37 s; (default, 5) 429.14 s / 237 MB avail-mem floor / peak load 8.13; (pinned, 4) **444.69 s / 417 MB / 6.32** ← shipped; (pinned, 5) 403.55 s / 337 MB / 6.41. **Pinning is a contention knob, not a speed knob** — worth 0.07 % of wall-clock at 4 workers, but it stops every worker sizing its BLAS pool from the core count, dropping peak load 8.13 → 6.41 and raising the memory floor, and it is what makes more workers actually pay (4→5 buys 3.4 % unpinned, 9.2 % pinned). 5 workers therefore stays one env var away (`JB_TEST_WORKERS=5`) rather than default: this Jetson also runs the robot, and its swap is **zram** and already ~50 % used, so over-commit degrades hard and an OOM-killed worker surfaces as a confusing "node down". The **`serial` marker set was re-derived from the contract rather than from observed failures**, after an audit caught the lazy first pass — grepping for process-global measurements yields 5 tracemalloc/GC tests plus 2 CasADi wall-clock solve-time budgets (~28 s tail), the last two of which no failure had nominated. Also adds `--strict-markers`, closing the sibling silent-no-op so a typo'd marker fails loudly instead of selecting nothing. | [2026-07-31-test-suite-parallelisation](2026-07-31-test-suite-parallelisation.md) |
| 2026-07-31 | resolved | CAN3 instability root-cause hunt (continuation of the 07-29 flap) | **The CAN3 wire errors were the bridge's own analog drive path failing under load — software exonerated by exhaustion; Jugglebot now runs on the CAN2 controller.** Exclusion ladder on deployed-state deltas, one element per rung: poller boots-OFF v6 (errors with ZERO poller frames on the ≤7-frame boot version sweep), USB unplugged, bridge **FW3 rollback** (clean-era binary reproduces within seconds), Platform Teensy **V1 rollback** flashed via the Arduino IDE (toolchain-clean A/B per the platformio.ini compile-gate discipline), a different hand ODrive, the Platform Teensy removed entirely (termination kept ~60 Ω), toolchain pinned (teensy@5.1.0, package mtimes May–June) — the fault survived every rung. **Breaking asymmetry**: RX at the bridge perfect throughout (7/7 heartbeats fresh, Platform-Teensy 2 Hz report clean end-to-end, REC ≈ 0, ACK pulses working) while ONLY the bridge's own full-frame CAN3 TX died bit1-dominant — a weak transmitter, not a sick cable; worst boot: `tec_inc_sum` 269 365, one BUS_OFF latch, FlexCAN's infinite hardware retransmit = the observed "once started, never stops". **Conviction by role swap** (2-line FlexCAN instance change + ESR1 base addresses + physical plugs): Jugglebot loom on CAN2 = flawless including a full reload session; cone on CAN3 = clean under sustained 100 Hz 0x7DD (light load); the same CAN3 path against the 8-node chain minutes earlier = catastrophic ⇒ **load-dependent drive-path fault** (transceiver output stage or resistive joint, hub-connector↔Teensy stretch), progressively worsening 07-29→31. **Landed (FW 6→7)**: the swap becomes the operating config (wire names stay role-keyed); poller boots ON again (Phase 7 runbook premise restored; FIX C dismissed — rate was never the problem); cone BusHealth added to `HeartbeatT2J.flags` bits 4-5 (no payload change, no PROTOCOL_VERSION bump, UNKNOWN=0 self-describing) → `/link_status` `bus3_health` → GUI health dot live with the row-grey/health-dot rendering split fixed; GUI relabeled to the physical truth (CAN2 = Jugglebot core with the role-keyed charts, CAN3 = cone, health-only); Platform Teensy gains a deterministic two-Teensy pio flash path (`-w`, no `-s`, `--mcu=TEENSY40`, button-press selects) and is reflashed V2 (decel FF) via pio. Verification: `pytest tests/firmware tests/ros -q`, run 2026-07-31: **1931 passed in 277.30 s**; xlang wire digest unchanged (**40 passed in 0.18 s**); `bus3_health='OK'` probed end-to-end on `/link_status`. OPEN: repair the CAN3 path then revert the swap; the substitute hand ODrive needs `gpio2_mode=1` + fw 0.6.11 if it stays; cone PROFILE slot (traffic charts); re-check the ERR_TIMEOUT epidemic's incidence on CAN2 — plausibly the same dying path all along. **Same-evening postscripts**: the v5 bump's first flash shipped a stale-header v4 binary (incremental build) → silent-and-total link darkness; clean-rebuild + ON-WIRE version-byte verify is now standing practice after any PROTOCOL_VERSION bump. **CORRECTION — the "ODrives went silent as a group" observations were a display misread**: a temporary per-cmd histogram proved heartbeats (7×10 Hz) + full telemetry flowing continuously throughout; `[axes]` `*` = active-error mark (uniform 48-V-off undervoltage, documented benign) and the number is heartbeat age in **ms**, not a count — the drive-path conviction rests on the bridge's TX evidence and stands. **Platform-Teensy pio image is CAN-MUTE** (board enumerates as "Teensy 4.1" despite being a 4.0 — 4.1-flavoured build leakage suspected): pio uploads SUSPENDED in platformio.ini (buttonless machinery retained, disabled); an Arduino-IDE V2 reflash restored the relay conduit (`platform_fw_version: 2`). And a **Foxy logger landmine** (one line logging two severities in `_set_mpc_active`) crashed the node on the first STANDBY arm — the safe-shutdown stow behaved exactly as designed — fixed with distinct call sites + the one sibling (`ball_butler_node.py:575`). | [2026-07-31-can3-drive-path-fault-jugglebot-to-can2](2026-07-31-can3-drive-path-fault-jugglebot-to-can2.md) |
| 2026-07-29 | resolved | Hand ball-present sensor — post-flash CAN3 regression (FW_VERSION 4) | **CAN3 sat error-passive 42.4 % of a session in which the robot was never commanded — and the gate meant to protect the bus was the amplifier.** Bag `2026-07-29_22-37-06`, robot idle all 83.5 s: `bus1_health` WARN 354/834 samples, **426 transitions / 213 full cycles**, WARN runs median 100 ms (max 900), **zero BUS_OFF**, BB CAN1 OK ×834, UDP `crc_errors`/`decode_errors` 0. **Key correction that reframed severity**: WARN does **not** key on the error-*warning* level — `can_buses.h:196-198` keys on **error-PASSIVE (TEC or REC ≥ 128)** and documents that 96 was deliberately rejected (a normal 12 V ramp peaks at REC ≈ 121), so 128 costs ~16 failed transmissions at +8 each. Staleness excluded structurally (480 OK samples *require* RX fresher than the 2 s `CAN_HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_US`, so every WARN is a live ESR1 `flt_live`); Teensy reboot excluded (`uptime_ms` monotonic, steps `{100: 832, 300: 1}`); Jetson excluded (first OK→WARN at **t = 0.089 s**, the first sample, bridge already up 755 s). **Controls are the spine**: 11 pre-flash sessions all **0.0 % WARN**, including **six in the identical idle state** (kills "idle ⇒ weak decay pump ⇒ flap" as sufficient) and one at **759 s uptime** vs tonight's 755 (kills fresh-boot transient); platform-Teensy fw 2 and the hand `gpio2_mode` NVM change both sit on the clean side. `git diff b3c82f7..HEAD` over the bridge = 15 files, every one traced: **the only new CAN3 frames are `0x0C4`/`0x0C5`, the FW4 50 Hz hand-sensor poll**. Poll grid measures exactly **49.999 slots/s**, but only **1205/4164 slots (28.94 %) get a reply**, 89 gaps ≥ 240 ms, max 980 ms. **Layer 1 ESTABLISHED**: the poll drives sustained error-passive and `jugglebot_commands_allowed` (`can_buses.cpp:760-767`) converts it into a system-wide ~42 %-duty outage — `platform_relay`, `hand_ops` (the catch stroke), leg/config RPCs, homing, version-check, and the poller itself (`gpio_poll.cpp:247`), which is the **positive-feedback limit cycle**: refusing TX removes the 0x7DD decay pump that `can_buses.h:200-202` names outright. Coupling measured: P(stale ⏐ WARN) 0.223 vs 0.052 OK, **RR 4.29×**, φ **+0.256** confined to L = 0/+1 (a shared *fast* mechanism), mean reply age +80.0 ms in WARN. **φ ≈ 0.26, not 1** — half the OK-window slots also miss, so the transaction fails beyond the gate. **Layer 2 OPEN by construction**: which wire-error class raises TEC/REC is unanswerable from a 90 s bag because the per-class counters are computed at 1 kHz and **discarded to a serial console** (`can_buses.h:269-270`). **The prime suspect died**: H-A concurrent FlexCAN TX killed twice — every write already runs in a PRIMASK critical section atomic against tasks *and* ISRs (`can_buses.cpp:659-732`), and `setpoints_sent = 0` means the 500 Hz interp writer, the only concurrent writer that exists, was **silent all session**. Also killed: malformed frame (wire form byte-identical to BB's production `requestArbitraryParameter`; endpoint 726 confirmed empirically, raw word tracked the physical ball 622 held / 212 empty; ACK precedes acceptance filtering anyway), FIFO overflow (does not touch TEC/REC/FLTCONF), bench-sysid build (50 Hz grid + 99.62 Hz telem, no 250 Hz topic anywhere), `0x0C4` duplicate-ID (platform analyser's `timingID` is `0x004`, RX-filter only), and "poller isn't flashed" — that last came from a **stale plan cell** and misled a sub-investigation. **Good news**: `leg_interp`'s 500 Hz setpoint TX calls `can_jugglebot_send` **directly** and bypasses the gate, so the safety-critical path is untouched in both directions. **Latent chain found, did not fire**: every WARN→OK edge dispatches the Jetson's conservative cold-start `STATE_READ` (`teensy_bridge_node.py:1765-1776`), itself gate-refusable, whose fallback sets `is_homed = False` (`:3097-3116`) — **a flapping CAN3 can make the robot forget it is homed** (bag: `cold_start_is_homed = '1'` ×834, but 213 edges in 83 s is thin margin). **FIX 0** (zero code, zero flash): `gpio_poll off` restores exactly FW3 CAN3 behaviour — **trap: `gpio_poll_init` boots ON (`gpio_poll.cpp:190`), the toggle does not survive a reboot**. **FIX A** is the class-closing contract: a **second** classifier `classify_command_gate()` gating on **sustained** confinement (`CAN_PASSIVE_SUSTAIN_US = 1.0 s`, BUS_OFF still instant, FW 4→5), with `classify_bus_health` left **byte-unchanged on purpose** — adding hysteresis there would hide the flap from `/link_status` in the same commit that stops it hurting, while layer 2 is still open. Threshold sized by **physics not the observed distribution** (at the 100 Hz floor, TEC 160→127 takes ~330 ms, so still-passive-at-1 s is not self-healing; and 1 s stays under the 2 s staleness term). **Tradeoff named**: FIX A widens the command window on a degrading bus — accepted because error-passive nodes still transmit, FlexCAN retransmits, callers have ack/timeout paths, `partner_recent` still refuses a partner-less bus, and leg setpoints were **already** ungated there, so it is strictly less exposure than the status quo. Control walk: **no TX timing changes**, one MPC cycle is bit-identical. **FIX B** uplinks the counters (additive MsgType `0x8C`, no PROTOCOL_VERSION bump). **FIX C deliberately deferred** behind the bench A/B discriminator table (`tecInc` vs `recInc` vs `ack=`/`bit1=`/`stuff=`), which **must run on the current FW4 before the fix is flashed**. Untested-but-recorded, with an earlier draft's error corrected: gate refusals return **`ERR_BUS_DOWN`** (`rpc.cpp:117`), **not** `ERR_TIMEOUT` (`rpc.cpp:118`, a `can_jugglebot_send` failure FIX A does not touch and **may raise**, since more commands now reach the send path) — so the open epidemic must first be attributed to a code before FIX A can be tested against it; 10 Hz sampling being blind to sub-100 ms excursions is what lets 0.0 % sampled WARN coexist with a real epidemic. **Safety verdict — with the poller ON do NOT run a catching or throwing session**: not dangerous (refusal, not misbehaviour; no faults, no clamps, no BUS_OFF) but operationally broken, since catching depends on `hand_ops`. With `gpio_poll off`: safe, FW3-equivalent. **Verification** — `pio run -e teensy41` + `-e teensy41_bench_sysid`, run 2026-07-29: both SUCCESS; `pytest tests/firmware -q`, run 2026-07-29: **368 passed in 190.94 s**; `pytest tests/ros/ -q`, run 2026-07-29: **1563 passed in 85.63 s** (1552 before); `pytest tests/ -q`, run 2026-07-29/30 overnight: **4294 passed, 3 xfailed in 1410.34 s**. **Addendum 2026-07-30/31**: the pre-registered A/B ran **NULL** (all counters frozen, all three arms — the condition is intermittent on an hours scale); same-arbitration-ID contention **exhaustively excluded** (single-transmitter proofs for 0x7DD/0x0C4 incl. the Platform Teensy's full git history; the three genuine shared-ID pairs rate/window-killed; `bad_axis` +2/s = **exactly** the Platform Teensy's 2 Hz 0x7DF report = wire-proof no foreign 0x7DD under SRX_DIS; foreign-0x0C4 blind spot named); operator physical inspection clean (CANH/CANL 66 Ω ≈ nominal 60); **v6 `cae3e6e` TEMP boots the poller OFF** (FW 5→6, runtime default only — serial `gpio_poll on|off` survives for the live-toggle arm; Phase 7 runbook premise suspended until the 6→7 revert); and the first poller-silent boot **reproduced the fault with zero poller frames**: the ≤7-frame Get_Version sweep (the boot's entire TX census — 0x7DD self-gates unanchored, no launch) collected ~18 failed attempts, bit1-dominant TX-context, TEC 146 error-passive, all counters frozen once the sustained gate closed. Layer-1 *limit-cycle* attribution stands; "poll traffic is the error *source*" **withdrawn** — the physical layer stands by elimination (prime suspect: the hand ODrive CAN stub, disturbed by the 2026-07-28 sensor install). Same boot, needs bench-state confirmation: all 7 ODrives heartbeated (counts 3–98) then went silent (`fresh=0/7`) while the Platform Teensy stayed clean at 2 Hz. Verification: `pio run -e teensy41 -t upload`, run 2026-07-31: SUCCESS (13.77 s), on-wire status `enabled=0`; `pytest tests/firmware -q`, run 2026-07-31: **368 passed in 191.34 s**. **CLOSED 2026-07-31**: root cause = the bridge's CAN3 analog drive path, load-dependent — see the 2026-07-31 entry; FIX C dismissed, poller boots ON again (FW7), Jugglebot runs on the CAN2 controller. | [2026-07-29-can3-bus-health-flap-hand-sensor-poller](2026-07-29-can3-bus-health-flap-hand-sensor-poller.md) |
| 2026-07-29 | in-progress | Hand ball-present sensor — Phase 6 (GUI pill + session runbook) | **The final software phase: the sensor reaches a human, twice.** A tri-state **ball-in-hand pill** in the System panel flags-grid (`flag-level-pill` precedent, driven from the existing `onHandTelemetry`): **filled** `HELD`, **hollow** `EMPTY`, greyed **DASHED** `UNKNOWN` — a *third look*, deliberately not a dimmed EMPTY, because fill/hollow is one visual axis and a faded hollow pill reads as "no ball, probably", collapsing at the last hop the distinction every layer below fought to keep. Flicker marker is a trailing `" ~"` in the **text** (single-encoded after review — the plan says "a flicker marker", singular; colourblind-safe and assertable from a DOM string; the first draft's amber ring deleted). The **latch semantics are the review-driven improvement**: set only on a valid sample whose raw bit disagrees with an *unchanged* verdict, cleared on any verdict change and any invalid sample — so the ~100 ms disagreement accompanying **every** ordinary edge (Phase 3's 5-sample debounce doing its job) never wears the marker, and a persisting `~` is unambiguous mid-carry spotty contact. A diagnostic that fires on the healthy path is worse than none: it trains the reader to discard the channel. 1000 ms hold (50 Hz sensor vs 100 ms GUI throttle — instantaneous would be unseeable). **Two blindness paths, two mechanisms**: `ball_held_valid` covers a dead **bridge** (node still publishing, vouching honestly), a **1 s message-stop watchdog** (10× throttle, `setMocapConnected` idiom) covers a dead **publisher** — the disconnect-branch reset deleted as redundant. Initial DOM state is **born UNKNOWN** in `initFlagsGrid`, forced by Phase 5 open question #4 (`/hand_telemetry` publishes nothing until the bridge's first `Telemetry` frame). New **7-test tripwire** `TestBallPillFieldNameContract` pins `ball_held`/`_raw`/`_valid` in **both** the `.msg` and `panels.js`, incl. a `\b(?!_)` lookahead so the bare verdict can't pass on its own prefix — rosbridge JSON means a rename yields `undefined` → false → **UNKNOWN forever, silently**, indistinguishable from a dead sensor during the one sitting that reads this pill; mutation-verified. Plus `tests/hardware/session_hand_ball_sensor.md` (**952 lines**, `session_phase7_reload.md` pattern) running the plan’s five Phase 7 steps as **six runbook steps** (bring-up inserted as step 3, so plan 3/4/5 = runbook 4/5/6). **Both BLOCKING findings came from the operator-safety lens** and neither was reachable from correctness/conformance: a motion-framing contradiction (fixed by inserting a bring-up step — launch → home → activate → confirm hold) and step 6 needing an explicit safe state before **every** hand-in-cup action, not once per step (hand axis is energised whenever ACTIVE; deactivate → seat → re-activate preferred). **Tooling honesty** as a runbook principle: step 4 states outright that true request→reply RTT is measurable by **nothing shipped** (the send time is recorded nowhere) — what IS measurable is inter-reply intervals from distinct `ball_held_stamp` values (mode ≈20 ms) + valid/stale/miss fractions, which sizes the provisional 240 ms `REPLY_STALE_US`; true RTT is an operator decision (firmware counter vs CAN analyser). Never let a step imply a measurement the system cannot make. Also baked in: `ros2 topic echo --once` **does not exist** on this Foxy box (older runbooks are wrong here), the bridge USB cable was **not connected** at authoring time (blocking precondition for steps 1/2/5), no soft-reboot exists so the banner recipe is `pio run -e teensy41 -t upload -t monitor` (**`-e` is load-bearing** — two envs, no `default_envs`, so an `-e`-less flash lands the NEVER-flash bench-sysid binary LAST and every v4 gate passes identically on it; `default_envs = teensy41` now added too), and ABORT-park leaves the bridge v4-with-poller-silent (v3-identical CAN3 load) but **not persistently** — re-apply after every reboot. **END-OF-PLAN FULL-SUITE GATE green**: `python -m pytest tests/ -q` **4288 passed, 3 xfailed in 1412.22 s**, reconciling 4271 + 10 + 7. Live as served (static files, no build step, hard-refresh only); browser confirmation of the four states is the runbook's pre-flight P3, **deferred to the operator — a real deviation from Phase 6's Done-when**, because no headless check in this repo can render `panels.js`. | [2026-07-29-hand-sensor-gui-pill-runbook](2026-07-29-hand-sensor-gui-pill-runbook.md) |
| 2026-07-29 | in-progress | Hand ball-present sensor — Phase 5 (ROS surface) | **The sensor reaches ROS as a tri-state.** `HandTelemetryMessage` gains exactly four appended fields (`ball_held` debounced, `ball_held_raw`, `ball_held_valid`, `builtin_interfaces/Time ball_held_stamp`); `teensy_bridge_node` subscribes to `MsgType.HAND_SENSOR` and caches the frame + a **Jetson-monotonic** RX stamp under `self._lock`. The load-bearing part is the **two-hop validity gate**: `ball_held_valid` requires the frame's own `VALID` **and** `TIME_SYNCED` **and** an RX age ≤ **3.0 s** on the host monotonic clock (≈3 keepalives) — never against `t_bridge_us`, a foreign wall clock that can step. Neither hop suffices alone: flags say "my cache is good" but can't see a dead link (the publisher is a free-running 100 Hz timer over cache, so flags-only republishes `held, valid` forever unplugged); RX-age says "I heard from the bridge" but a failed poller keepalives punctually with `¬VALID`. Never-seen ⇒ all-False + zeroed stamp; seen-but-invalid **retains** its bits and stamp (the "what was it, how old?" diagnostic — the flag is the protection, wiping only changes which wrong answer a broken consumer gets). `/link_status` gains `hand_ball_sensor` = `held\|empty\|unknown\|stale miss=N raw=0x········` in **lowercase** hex matching the bridge console's `%08lx` (Phase 7 step 2 compares them textually); `empty` reachable only from a valid reading, never-seen renders `unknown (never seen)` rather than fabricating zeros. 10 tests on the paired-node harness; the review catch worth naming is the **inverted active-low raw-word constants** — held ⇒ bit 2 **CLEAR**, empty ⇒ SET — inert to every assertion but the artifact Phase 7 operators read to learn the toggle direction. Same shape as the fix for the BallButler fail-open reload-skip defect (age-only, no vouching term). **Not deployed** — needs `colcon build --packages-select jugglebot_interfaces jugglebot` + relaunch. | [2026-07-29-hand-sensor-ros-surface](2026-07-29-hand-sensor-ros-surface.md) |
| 2026-07-29 | in-progress | Hand ball-present sensor — Phase 4 (additive uplink message) | **Small phase: Phase 3's snapshot reaches the wire.** New `HandSensor` message at MsgType **0x8B** (next free slot in the reserved 0x89–0x8F telemetry block), 14 B packed exactly per plan (`u64 t_bridge_us` wall-at-last-good-reply, `u32 raw_states` verbatim, `u8 flags`, `u8 miss_count`), emitted by `hand_sensor_uplink_step()` in `telemetry.cpp` beside the hand cmd-echo (no new TU), called from `task_telem`. **Additive ⇒ no `PROTOCOL_VERSION` bump** (LegCmd precedent — the two ends deploy in either order); `test_wire_layout_frozen` re-pinned in the same change (`5ad4fa88cf2b…` → `1e1cf7314377…`), version pin untouched at 4. Three review fixes shape it: `flags` promoted to a **generated `HandSensorFlags` enum** because `test_wire_layout_frozen` hashes field `name:type` and **not comments**, so a literals-plus-prose bitset could let producer and consumer drift with the suite green (`HeartbeatT2JFlags` precedent; Phase 5 reads the same names); a `static_assert` pinning `CHECK_INTERVAL_MS × TELEM_RATE_HZ ≥ 1000`, since nothing else bound poll-period ≥ telem-tick and a Phase 7 retune below it would silently coalesce the raw per-sample bit that approved decision #4 requires reach ROS; and the `controller/teensy_link` re-export landing in **both** `protocol.py` and `__init__.py` (both prior additive messages did — `teensy_bridge_node` imports from the package root, which Phase 5 needs). Freshness is an **equality test** on the reply's wall stamp, never an interval (a wall-anchor step costs one redundant frame, not a wrong verdict), so the wire rate is the **poll** rate 50 Hz, not the 100 Hz telemetry rate; a 1 Hz keepalive makes staleness observable rather than silent. Deliberately **not** gated on `JBBallDetect::ENABLED` — a kill-switched build still keepalives honest-UNKNOWN, so "sensor compiled out" ≠ "no bridge at all". **Not flashed** — ships with Phase 3 at Phase 7 step 1. | [2026-07-29-hand-sensor-uplink-message](2026-07-29-hand-sensor-uplink-message.md) |
| 2026-07-29 | in-progress | Hand ball-present sensor — Phase 3 (bridge firmware poller) | **The phase where the qualified endpoint id finally leaves the Teensy.** New TU `Teensy_code_canbridge/gpio_poll.{h,cpp}` on `task_homing` (100 Hz) beside `version_check_step`: a two-phase non-blocking request/await machine polling hand node 6 with an RxSdo `OPCODE_WRITE` of `EndpointId::odrive_pro_0_6_11::get_gpio_states`, decoding the TxSdo `uint32` through a new typed decoder (the dead C++ float32 one deleted; the `_u32` suffix **kept** because Python's same-named decoder has float semantics). Every normative semantic implemented and behaviourally tested (console rendering excepted): tri-state UNKNOWN, timeout advances staleness only (never the verdict, never `miss_count`), saturating-and-frozen `miss_count`, asymmetric 5-sample HELD→EMPTY debounce, first-ever-EMPTY ⇒ EMPTY, and a **stale-gap reseed** so a pre-outage HELD cannot survive a 30 s outage for even 100 ms. Three-state `Get_Version` gate — UNRECEIVED ⇒ quiet/no fault, MATCH ⇒ poll, MISMATCH ⇒ latched park printed at 1 Hz — which **explicitly amends** `version_check.h`'s "ZERO version SEMANTICS in firmware": the wrong-build id *answers plausibly*, so the refusal must precede the RxSdo leaving the Teensy, and only firmware can do that. Publication is a PRIMASK critical section, **not** the house seqlock: the reader (`PRIO_UDP_TX 3`) outranks the writer (`PRIO_HOMING 2`), so a preempting reader would spin against a writer that can never be scheduled — livelock, not retry. Stamps captured at **reply arrival** (a review fix; stamping at the consumption tick biases `t_bridge_us` 7–9 ms late, material inside the pre-registered ~35 ms sigma). Runtime `gpio_poll on\|off` toggle required building the bridge's first serial **input** path; the one BLOCKING finding was the off-path failing to drain the state machine, which would republish a mid-off-window reply as fresh on re-enable. Kill switch proven by build matrix — `enabled:false` text returns to the exact 226624 baseline. `FW_VERSION` 3→4; two test layers (xref source-scan + 13-case/100-assertion native behavioural on the real TU), both mutation-validated. **Not flashed** — Phases 3+4 ship in one flash at Phase 7. | [2026-07-29-hand-sensor-bridge-gpio-poller](2026-07-29-hand-sensor-bridge-gpio-poller.md) |
| 2026-07-29 | in-progress | Hand ball-present sensor — Phase 2 (endpoint-id contract) | **The repo's ODrive endpoint ids were S1 values in a flat table with nothing saying "S1".** Jugglebot's hand is a Pro, where `get_gpio_states` is 726, not 700 — and 700 on a Pro is `encoder_estimator1.status`, a read-only byte that **answers** well-formed TxSdo replies, so the wrong id reads as a live sensor that never changes and "no reply ⇒ wrong endpoint" commissioning is worthless. Fixed as a contract, not a `700→726` edit: `protocol_config.yaml` endpoints restructured into (board, fw)-qualified groups (`odrive_pro_0_6_11.get_gpio_states: 726`, `odrive_s1_0_6_11.get_gpio_states: 700` + `commutation_mapper_pos_abs: 488`), both emitters taught the nested shape (`EndpointId::odrive_pro_0_6_11::get_gpio_states`, `ENDPOINT_ODRIVE_PRO_0_6_11_GET_GPIO_STATES`), every consumer migrated in the same change. 488 moves **verbatim** (production-proven on BB's S1); the Pro's 461 is deliberately **not** emitted — the contract qualifies ids by evidence, not table completeness. Jugglebot's dangling `commutation_mapper.pos_abs` is **deleted, not re-pointed**: re-pointing would hand an S1 id to a Pro-facing module, the exact class defect being closed, one line below the fix. Native golden regenerated (2 of 19 rows, `e801`→`d602`); new YAML→generated round-trip test pins codegen freshness *and* the name-mangling contract Phase 3 quotes. BallButler lands in two commits so `git blame` never blames the endpoint phase for the motor constant. | [2026-07-29-hand-sensor-endpoint-id-contract](2026-07-29-hand-sensor-endpoint-id-contract.md) |

| 2026-07-29 | in-progress | Hand ball-present sensor — Phase 0 (fw-version surfacing) | **The bridge decoded every axis's `Get_Version` reply and threw the numbers away.** `_version_check_poll` fed them to `record_version` / `validate_group`; the PASS log line printed no versions and `/link_status` had no ODrive-version row (only `platform_fw_version`, which is the *Platform Teensy*). Phase 0 renders them — `_odrive_fw_versions_str()` appended as `(fw ...)` to **both** the PASS and FAIL firmware-check lines, plus a new `odrive_fw_versions` KeyValue distinct from `platform_fw_version` — **including `Get_Version`'s fourth byte (`fw_unreleased`, previously discarded as `_unrel`)**, the only wire evidence that could carry the `-1` of `0.6.11-1`. This matters because the sensor plan pins RxSdo endpoint id **726** against the hand ODrive **Pro** at fw `0.6.11`/`0.6.11-1`, and neither fallback is free: `validate_group` checks firmware only for *internal consistency within a hardware group*, so "firmware check PASSED" would pass seven axes agreeing on the WRONG version; and the id the repo carries today (**700**, the S1 value) exists on Pro 0.6.11 as `encoder_estimator1.status`, answering plausibly-wrong with **no timeout**. Approved-decisions row 6 owns the byte; empirical confirmation is Phase 7 step 1. Rendering decisions are both about not lying: absent axes render `axis:?` rather than being dropped (a partial bench rig legitimately sweeps fewer axes), and the fourth byte renders **even when zero**, so "the drive reports no suffix" stays distinguishable from "the bridge doesn't surface it". `MotorStateTracker` is untouched — the byte lives in a node-local `_fw_unreleased` dict, because the tracker has callers outside this node and `validate_group` has no use for it; validation and evidence-rendering stay separate. **The renderer must never fabricate a `-0`**: on any future path that populated the tracker without the byte, a `.get(axis, 0)` default would read as measured evidence ("the drive reports no suffix") for exactly the byte row 6 exists to confirm — so the renderer is total and renders `-?` for a missing byte instead (an earlier direct-indexing draft's "loud `KeyError`" theory was refuted by the pre-commit audit: the exception would go dark inside `_publish_link_status`'s blanket `except` or kill the node from the 1 Hz timer). `_fw_unreleased[axis]` is written BEFORE `record_version`, which keeps `-?` unreachable today (the version-poll and `link_status` timers share the node-default `MutuallyExclusiveCallbackGroup`, so no concurrent read exists — the ordering makes it hold if that changes). The plan's "Done when" test is split in two because a partial rig and a PASS/FAIL log line are mutually exclusive by construction — `_version_check_poll` returns at the `all_jugglebot_versions_received()` gate before either log call — so the partial-rig test asserts the shared renderer plus the `/link_status` row, the surface that exists in that state. A reviewer's throttled "sweep incomplete" log line was rejected because `/link_status` already carries the row at 10 Hz (the Phase 6 runbook points there). No behaviour changes: nothing new is asserted, refused or faulted on. Three parallel read-only reviewers (correctness / plan-conformance / minimalism), **zero blocking findings**, six fixes applied at finalize. `python -m pytest tests/ros/test_teensy_bridge_node_version.py -q`, run 2026-07-29: **8 passed in 2.15 s**. `python -m pytest tests/ -q`, run 2026-07-29: **4256 passed, 3 xfailed in 1421.88 s (0:23:41)**. **Nothing has run on powered hardware** — whether the hand Pro reports a non-zero fourth byte is unknown until Phase 7 step 1. Deploy: `colcon build --packages-select jugglebot` + `source install/setup.bash` + relaunch (the launch runs the INSTALLED copy); no interfaces rebuild, no firmware flash. | [2026-07-29-hand-sensor-fw-version-surfacing](2026-07-29-hand-sensor-fw-version-surfacing.md) |
| 2026-07-25 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — Phase F (TossContinuous, the programme finale) | **Repetition ships as a SEQUENCER, and the instrument that gates it was validated on one side only.** `TossContinuous` runs `num_throws` toss-catch cycles from one goal with a configurable dwell; every cycle is an ordinary `Toss` built by `_build_toss_cycle` and ticked by `_run_toss_cycle` — the same two methods the single `Toss` now uses after they were extracted — so a session **cannot** drift from the toss the hardware ladder validated. A pure-Python outer FSM owns only *when* the next cycle starts, *whether* it starts, and the accounting; `stop_on_miss` defaults TRUE on the wire (verified against the real generated message, not just the mock). The dwell is a QUIESCENT WAIT, not a stretched `throw_delay`, because the rejected option leaves `catch/armed` RAISED over a loaded cup for the whole gap with `catch_coordinator`'s reactive path live — verified in the mechanism (`_on_throw_announcement` and `_prime_retry_tick` are both gated on `_catch_armed`). **The brief's 2.0 s dwell floor was REFUTED, not adopted**: it needs `throw_delay <= 1.458 s`, **2.042 s below** the toss FSM's own `MIN_TOSS_THROW_DELAY_S` (3.49 rejects / 3.50 dispatches against the real FSM); shipped floor is derived, `4.10 s` absolute and `5.60 s` at the default delay, and a short dwell is REFUSED rather than silently stretched. **THE CONVERGENT FINDING — two lenses, and it would have burned the sitting**: the CS-1..CS-6 trace checker this phase ships as the bench gate had only ever been shown clean on an **all-CAUGHT** session, while runbook `CONT-STEP-1` makes an **all-MISSED** dry trace the MANDATORY capture *before any ball flies*. That ladder publishes `catch/armed False` FIRST and only then retracts, so the cycle's own mandated retract — **~10 rev** on a dry trace, since no catch stroke re-parks the hand — lands inside the dwell window CS-3 measures. Building the missing `cont_dry` case found two real defects in sequence: CS-3's hand bound was **DT-5's idle-window `0.05 rev`**, which is **1.12x** the worst real post-disarm residual (`0.0446 rev`, measured over the 16 toss-shaped gaps of `temp/logs/toss_trace_2026-07-27_15-39-50.jsonl`) — a coin-flip false STOP on a row the runbook marks a hard STOP; and the first repair, anchoring on the first in-park sample, **still FAILed `cont_dry`** because the `0.5 rev` park band is wide enough that the tail of the retract ramp is already inside it (baseline `0.250` vs settled `0.000`). Landed formulation is **no-ascent, running minimum**, at a measured `0.15 rev` (3.4x the worst real ascent, 10x below `ASCENT_POS_REV`, 66x below the auto-prime ascent it exists to catch), plus a new failure for a window where the hand never reaches park. Validated three ways: `cont_dry` 6/6 PASS, the new `viol_cs3_hand` FAILs CS-3 with zero collateral, **16/16 real windows pass at 3.4x**. **THE EXTRACTION REGRESSED THE SINGLE TOSS, in the direction nobody expected**: `_execute_toss`'s `rclpy`-shutdown branch terminalised NOTHING on the goal handle at HEAD; the discarded `exit_kind` let it fall through to `goal_handle.abort()`, so a status transition on a dying executor could overwrite the `ABORTED_SHUTDOWN` line with `ABORTED_EXCEPTION` and turn a clean Ctrl-C into a fault trace. The *session* handled it correctly and its test asserted parity with "the single Toss does the same" — which the same change had made false; the pre-existing shutdown test asserted the outcome and the safing but never the handle, so it passed both ways. Fixed and now pinned on the single Toss. **THE DWELL FLOOR SIZED ONLY THE HANDOFF THAT COMMANDS NOTHING**: `dwell_margin_s = 0.6` covers the CAUGHT handoff (a verdict, two ticks). A MISSED cycle the session continues past hands over through a whole `SAFE_ABORT` ladder whose every rung returns on a **SERVICE ACK** — `_go_home()` returns when a `2.0 s` recentre profile has been *installed*, `_retract_hand_with_retries()` on the first ack — so at the shipped defaults the naive arithmetic starts cycle N+1 **1.7 s before the recentre lands**, and the docstring's "lateness is absorbed" was true of the FSM and false of the session. Landed `DEFAULT_SESSION_MISS_CLEANUP_S = 2.80 s` as a FLOOR (`CATCH_CONFIRM_WINDOW_S 0.7` + `go_home 2.0` + 2 ticks) that can only lengthen a gap, chosen over a `stop_on_miss`-dependent dwell floor because that would have REJECTED the all-defaults configuration the mandatory dry trace uses. Also: **`REJECTED_THROW_DELAY`**, without which the advertised `4.10 s` absolute floor was an arithmetic identity (a `throw_delay 2.0 / dwell 3.0` goal was ACCEPTED, built a whole cycle's state, then died `REJECTED_CANT_MAKE_LEAD` naming a field the operator never set); `catch/reach_center` removed from CS-3's silence set (published in the SAME FSM tick as the window's own end boundary, **131 us** apart on the real bag — CS-4 already covers the whole gap more strictly, so no coverage is lost); CS-6's docstring corrected to what it proves (release-vs-previous-landing ordering, **not** the requested cadence — a collapse from 8 s to 2 s would PASS); and `ball_possession_contract.md` § 7.1's *"no edit to the coordinator"* upgrade claim **refuted** (cycle N+1's `ball_seated` is a plain read of the sticky latch, `_on_balls` can only ever SET it True, and `judge()` is reachable only from a `/balls` CAUGHT while the tracker prunes the track ~2 s in against a 4.10 s floor) and replaced with the two coordinator edits retention actually needs. **Deferred with the trace recorded**: the unprotected pre-`try` region of `_execute_toss_continuous` (NOT-PROVEN reachability by both reviewers, pre-existing in `_execute_toss`, and the safe restructure needs `session is None` guards on two handlers). `python tools/probes/toss_trace_synth.py --all --verify`, run 2026-07-29: **30/30 OK, matrix CLEAN** (RED at HEAD on `viol_dt7`, a pre-existing vacuity repaired here). `pytest tests/ -q`, run 2026-07-29: **4254 passed, 3 xfailed in 1435.48 s (0:23:55)** (+114 on the `3332bc6` baseline of 4140 — accounted EXACTLY: `--collect-only` reports 70 tests in `test_toss_session.py` and 44 in `test_toss_continuous_node.py`, both new files; xfail unchanged at 3, so no test was weakened to reach green). **NOT DEPLOYED, NOTHING HAS RUN** — `colcon build --packages-select jugglebot_interfaces jugglebot` + relaunch, **BOTH packages**: `reload_coordinator_node` imports `TossContinuous` at module scope, so a `jugglebot`-only build raises `ImportError` before the node is constructed and takes **all three** ball-op actions down. The review found four per-section runbook blocks affirmatively saying "no interfaces rebuild"; underneath them **every** build instruction in the file was the single-package command — all **20** swept. No firmware flash. § SECTION CONT runs LAST. | [2026-07-25-toss-continuous-action](2026-07-25-toss-continuous-action.md) |
| 2026-07-29 | resolved | Self-toss anomaly fixes — Phase E (sim gate seed bugfix) | **The Tier-8b asymmetry map was one trial replicated.** `_run_asymmetry_map` advanced its seed offset only AFTER the list comprehension that consumed it, so all `diag_trials` trials in a cell ran the IDENTICAL seed — which is why every published cell read `0/4` or `4/4` with `err_mean == err_worst` exactly. The other two seeded columns are unaffected (gating uses `cfg.seed + gi` per trial; contact-diag uses `+ i` with `i += 1`). Fixed to `+ i + k`. **The fix does NOT make the map trustworthy and the first draft of the comment wrongly said it did**: on the post-fix re-run **47 of 48 cells still read 0/4 or 4/4** (one reads 1/4), so the near-bimodality is the low-fidelity contact model, not a seeding artefact — a `0/4` cell is still not evidence of a 0 % direction. Consequence: the 2026-07-25 directional map is **refuted, not merely stale** (`+y` now 10/10 at 70 mm, 10/10 at 100, 9/10 at 150; NW 10/10 everywhere; only sub-9/10 cell is SW at the cap), so the plan's `+y`/NW ladder guidance gets a REFUTED banner and Phase E's cap ships as a simple scalar. Changes no shipped robot behaviour. | [2026-07-29-toss-gate-asymmetry-seed](2026-07-29-toss-gate-asymmetry-seed.md) |
| 2026-07-25 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — Phase E (displaced throws to ±150 mm) | **The reach envelope was bounding two different things with one number, and only one of them could be judged in time.** Four Tier-8b goals on 2026-07-27 were refused 4/4 — `catch target 146/131/117/117 mm from the armed hold pose exceeds the 80 mm reach envelope` — because the envelope, which exists to bound UNREQUESTED tracker drift, was centred on the pose held at arming and so also capped the REQUESTED `A→B` reach; and since that reach is published at `t_release`, its verdict could only ever arrive **after the irreversible commitment, with the ball airborne**. Closed as a class by contract **C-REACH-1**: the centre moves to the DECLARED catch point `B` (`catch/reach_center`, consumed-and-cleared at the arm raise), the 80 mm radius does not move, and the reload declares nothing so its path stays byte-identical. Throw site `A` is now the platform's LIVE commanded pose (`trajectory/commanded_position`); `toss_throw_site_mm` is RETIRED and `compute_release_state_tilted`'s site argument is REQUIRED — one enforcement point, because a config `A` is not merely a wrong number: POSITIONING obediently TRANSLATES the platform to it before throwing. Cap re-based on its own evidence to **150 mm**; CAUGHT now STAYS (`ACTION_STAY` = the RECENTER ladder minus `go_home`). **A LIVE HAZARD FOUND AT FINALIZE AND FIXED**: the new reload `REJECTED_NOT_CENTERED` gate used the bare 80 mm envelope as its tolerance, leaving **zero** budget for the reload's own pre-tilt swing — `compute_catch_orientation` CLAMPS at `MAX_TILT_DEG` for every real BB arrival (18–40° off vertical), so the centroid shift SATURATES at `64.78·sin(12°) = 13.47 mm` on every reload (measured invariant across 18/25/30/40°). Parks in `(66.5, 80]` mm were ADMITTED then rejected `WORKSPACE` **after `ACTION_SEND_THROW`**, with BB's countdown started and the ball unsavable — the DISP-7.1 E-STOP condition reached through a gate that said yes, and the ladder walks into it (DISP-3 catches at 70 mm and the next rung opens with a Reload). Tolerance is now `envelope − hand_catch_offset·sin(MAX_TILT_DEG)` = **66.53 mm**, derived not magic. **SHIPS WITH A KNOWN LIMITATION, DOCUMENTED NOT PAPERED OVER — two independent lenses converged on it**: chaining is refused AT the cap. The catch deliberately parks the CENTROID outside `B` so the CUP lands ON `B`; measured, the cup ends at exactly `(−150.00, 0)` and the centroid at `(−153.10, 0)`, and `trajectory/commanded_position` publishes the CENTROID — so the next goal reads `A = −153.10` and both surviving gates refuse (`REJECTED_DISPLACEMENT` back to centre, `REJECTED_WORKSPACE` for any other `B`). Root cause is a **frame** error, not an aim error (`A` is nominated, and POSITIONING makes it true): the wire supplies a centroid where `compute_release_state_tilted` documents a CUP xy — identical while level, divergent by `hand_catch_offset·sin(tilt)` = 2.07 % of displacement once `STAY` holds a receive tilt, crossing the box at `|B| ≈ 147 mm`. Not fixed because all three candidate fixes need an operator decision (publishing the cup xy needs the commanded ROTATION, which was deliberately kept off that wire to avoid C-LEVEL-1 double-correction; widening is bidirectional so it either strands harder or loosens an evidence-based cap; lowering the cap violates the ±150 ask). **The runbook was corrected so it cannot mis-route**: as reviewed, DISP-6.2 told the operator a `REJECTED_DISPLACEMENT` means *the throw site did not follow the platform* — the opposite of the truth. Also refuted at finalize: `REACH_*` limits were documented as *pinned by the drift-guard test* while nothing pinned them (harmless at the old 70 mm cap where the bound never bound; **this phase made it live**, so a cautious operator halving `leg_jerk_limit` would have had a 150 mm goal pass CHECKING at 171.5 mm against a real 85.8 mm budget) — now genuinely pinned; DISP-5.3 quoted the **platform-space** closed form (17578) as the expected reading of the **leg-space** `peak_leg_jerk`, measured 9742 max (0.55×); `throw_site_known` defaulted fail-OPEN in a family where every sibling is fail-CLOSED, and flipping it immediately caught a test silently relying on it. `pytest tests/ -q`, run 2026-07-29: **4140 passed, 3 xfailed in 1409.60 s (0:23:29)** (+44 on the 4096 baseline at `956a4b8`; xfail unchanged at 3). **NOT DEPLOYED** — `colcon build --packages-select jugglebot` + relaunch (row DISP-0 greps the installed copy); no flash, no interfaces rebuild. | [2026-07-25-displaced-throws-150](2026-07-25-displaced-throws-150.md) |
| 2026-07-29 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — Phase 7 (post-release decel) | **The hand touched its end stop because the only open-loop braking term was sized from a model that omits the motor's rotor.** `accelToTorque` models the axis as a pure translating mass on a spool, implying a reflected inertia of **7.3695e-6** kg·m² against a measured **≥1.0126e-5** — so the braking feedforward delivered **~70 %** of what the commanded decel physically needs, and the shortfall fell to a loop whose integrator unwind constant (0.100 s) is **1.1–2.1× the whole 47–93 ms ramp**. `calcThrow` gives that shortfall a lever arm: it allocates the decel **4.046 rev at every speed** (velocity-independent), ending exactly on `x3` with **zero** allowance for tracking error, so it converts 1:1 into end-stop travel — measured +0.074 / +0.063 / +0.347 / **+1.020** rev at 2.74 / 3.44 / 3.97 / 4.86 m/s. Fixed at ONE enforcement point (`throwDecelToTorque`, one caller), decel segment only; **commanded position and velocity are bit-identical on every kind**, so C-HAND-1's window, `_PRIME_INFLIGHT_S` and the timeline probe's verdict model stay valid without moving. Steepening was rejected on **measured authority**: the drive is NOT current-saturated (max |iq| 25.67 A of 50, achieved decel still growing 911→2330), yet the **commanded** decel at `FLIGHT_TIME_MAX_S` already sits at **83–86 %** of the axis's own 4178–4323 rev/s² ceiling. **REVIEW RE-ANCHORED THE SAFETY CLAUSE (two lenses converged):** the cited "accel-phase torque balance → 1.015e-5" is **ball-inclusive** — release is at `x2`, so the ball is in the cup for the whole ascent, and `INERTIA_RATIO = 0.747 = m_hand/(m_hand+m_ball)` makes it worth **2.412e-6, 24 %** of the figure. Ball-corrected it gives **7.74e-6**, *below* the declared 9.5e-6, so "7–10 % below BOTH identifications" was false; 7.74e-6 is *also* refuted as a decel inertia (it implies a flat η≈0.95 where **0.799** was measured). Re-anchored on a genuine **decel-side bound** `J ≥ (τ_FF+τ_grav)/(2π·a_ach) = 1.0126e-5` that uses **no `iq` measurement at all**, so the aliasing that killed a cascade model cannot corrupt it. **THE ONE-SIDED-SAFETY PROOF OMITTED GRAVITY, AND THERE IS NO CLEAN FIX**: on an upward decel gravity brakes in the SAME direction (+1.46e-6 of effective inertia at the band floor, +0.37e-6 at the ceiling), so open-loop the total *exceeds* `J_true` below `a_cmd ≈ 1900 rev/s²` and "can never over-brake" is false at the bottom of the band. Lowering the declared value to satisfy it needs `J_ff ≤ 8.69e-6` → a pessimistic peak of **10.80 rev**, past the 10.60 abort line. The two requirements are **incompatible**, which is the finding: the open-loop inequality is the wrong requirement. Enforcement moved to bench row **H7.4** plus a new **band-floor rung R0** — the only rung that tests the over-braking direction, and below every rung the ladder had, while routine reload work throws there daily. Tradeoff stated plainly rather than hidden behind the algebra. **THE VERDICT INSTRUMENT WAS READING THE WRONG PHASE OF THE STROKE**: `iq_ramp`'s window was anchored on the announcement's `throw_time`, which lands **120.5–166.9 ms** before the commanded stroke end while `t_dec` is only 52.7–93.3 ms ⇒ **zero overlap with the real decel on 13 of 17 tosses**; it reported the ASCENT current (10–23 A) where the true braking current is 3.6–9.9 A, and H7.5 gates the ladder's highest rung on it. Re-anchored on the commanded profile, `t_anchor` + fresh-sample count now printed. The probe also had **no self-check**, alone among the runbook's instruments; it now has a **two-sided `--self-check`** (pre-fix must FLAG, post-fix must ACCEPT) running through the real `analyse()` — mutation-verified: restoring the old window makes it FAIL. **AN UNEXAMINED DRIVE CLAMP COULD MAKE THE PHASE A NO-OP**: `torque_soft_min = −0.0551 N·m` = **exactly −10.00 A** against a `torque_soft_max` of +0.5; if live it truncates the feedforward above ~0.49 m. Probably not binding (achieved decel grows 2.6× across tiers, which a hard clamp forbids) but unresolved ⇒ pre-flight **H7.0c** reads it off the live drive *before* the flash, because otherwise the failure table would call a drive-config fault a physics result. Also: H7.3's flatness gate **0.25 → 0.35 rev** (0.25 sat at **95 %** of the model's own 0.237 prediction — a coin flip that blocks R5 on a working fix); `settle_offset` restated **per tier** (the single span silently dropped the 4.858 m/s tier, the one the phase exists for, where it is ~0); stale **"6055 rev/s²"** corrected at 3 surviving sites (it is the `MAX_EVENT_VEL_MPS` clamp value, not the band top's 3597 — as written it asserted a commanded decel *above* the axis's own ceiling); two sites still calling `platform_fw_version = 1` the flashed reading. `pytest tests/ -q`, run 2026-07-29: **4096 passed, 3 xfailed in 1428.61 s (0:23:48)** (+28 on the 4068 baseline at `ac74c1a` = this phase's two new files; xfail unchanged at 3). **NOT DEPLOYED** — Platform Teensy **flash** (`FW_VERSION` 1 → 2) *and* `colcon build` + relaunch, then the gated **R0→R5** ladder. | [2026-07-29-hand-post-release-decel](2026-07-29-hand-post-release-decel.md) |
| 2026-07-25 | tuned | Self-toss anomaly fixes — Phase C (seat-experiment prep) | **The seat rate becomes a config key, and the A/B that needs it gets an instrument.** Phase 4 left one question open — *does a non-zero seat rate widen the capture basin?* — and left it unanswerable, because moving the rate meant editing `planner.py` at the bench: invisible in the INSTALLED tree (so *which rate did that block run?* is unanswerable from the capture) and forgettable (so it ships as an accidental permanent default). Now `trajectory_op.catch_seat_rate_radps`, bound **once at import** into the same module attribute the 12 existing monkeypatches target — a call-time `hw.` lookup would take the whole C-CATCH-1 block vacuous **while staying green**, so that is pinned executably. Ships at **`0.0`**; commanded motion byte-identical. The A/B's central measurement — arrival offset — **had no runnable command anywhere in the repo** (the sitting computed it by hand, once), so `tools/probes/ball_arrival_offset.py` was built and validated two-sided against the real bag (worst error **0.6 mm**). Review then caught that the instrument's own acceptance was **vacuous on the one bias the design calls disqualifying** — a mutant with the post-contact cut deleted scored `8/8 PASS, exit 0`, because the synthetic bounce-out was grafted *below* the fit plane where the band filter drops it anyway; now grafted inside the band and mutation-verified both ways. Three protocol gates **fired on correct behaviour** and were re-derived from measured numbers: burn-in (`successive change < 10 mm` fires 3-in-7 on the settled plateau -> trend slope), matched arms (flat `5 mm` is 1.1-1.4 se at the expected sd, so identical aim fails 17-28 % of the time -> `max(5 mm, 2*se)`), and the marginal band (one-sided floor passes both under- and over-biased aim -> two-sided). A **refuted number** was corrected at its durable sites: the sitting's *"3.8 mm apart"* basin edge re-scores to **0.02 mm** — degenerate, not narrow, which is *why* the two hypotheses cannot be separated from that capture. Sitting deferred to the operator; the open prerequisite is how to bias BB aim +25-35 mm. `pytest tests/ -q` 2026-07-28: **4068 passed, 3 xfailed in 1422.44 s**. | [2026-07-25-seat-experiment-prep](2026-07-25-seat-experiment-prep.md) |

| 2026-07-27 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — hand-command-continuity Phase 6 | **The one deployment in the stack that failed silently — and the reviewers found the same defect class one layer up, in this phase's own code.** The Platform Teensy carried no version, so an un-flashed board was indistinguishable from a flashed one from the Jetson, and § CHECK HAND-4's fix is a **no-op on the clean path by design** — a stale board produces plausible rows that silently re-measure the pre-fix baseline. Worse on a *partly* stale tree, where `trajectory_node` does not exit and the promised row-B signature never appears. Rather than delete the claim — the same silent-deployment class this phase exists to close, one layer up — it was made TRUE: `INTERFACES_STALE` at construction, naming the missing fields and the rebuild command (logs, never raises: a refusal to start is the shape this phase argues must be earned). Traced independently: the verdict is captured only in `relay_read_robot_state` and re-read only on UDP-reconnect or CAN3-recovery **edges**, so a clean launch that missed its boot read stays `unknown` all session. All three reviewers offered the same code retry; **declined with reason** — it cannot read the version without also refreshing the cold-start cache, flipping `is_homed` False→True mid-session after the boot path already committed to the conservative fallback: a new homing discontinuity introduced to improve a diagnostic. Doc fix removes the false abort; code option recorded open. (3) The test pinning "warn, never refuse" **did not pin it** — it entered below `teensy_hand_traj_cmd`, the single funnel both hand services call and exactly where this repo's own one-enforcement-point convention would put a gate. Reproduced, then repaired **two-sided**: 26 pass clean, and the kind-3 assertion FAILS with a gate at the funnel. **REFUTED** the SRX_DIS hardening: "ignore a reply matching the last write" would discard *correct* reads, since the next honest read after `_write_is_homed` matches the written bytes by construction.
