| 2026-07-27 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — hand-command-continuity Phase 4 | **The velocity was sitting right there, declared and never read — and the two bounds that decide when honouring it is affordable were both wrong on the first pass.** `Trajectory.h::makeSmoothMove` seeded every hand profile `v = a = 0` from `current_hand_position` while `current_hand_velocity` sat declared `extern volatile` **two lines above it**, unread — so any command landing mid-motion commanded a velocity STEP. That unread `extern` IS the 2026-07-25 post-throw dip (catch arm at release+8-18 ms froze the setpoint at 6.20-7.78 rev with the hand through it at ~120 rev/s; coast to 10.17-10.32 rev, 0.775 rev off the guard; yank 0.34-1.75 rev = **10.7-55.3 mm** below the stroke end). Now `(x0, v0, a=0) → (target, 0, 0)`, decomposing EXACTLY into `pos = x0 + δ·s(τ) + (v0·T)·h(τ)`, `h = τ(1−τ)³(3τ+1)` — `h(0)=h(1)=0` so the live velocity never moves the endpoints, `h ≥ 0` so its whole effect is a one-sided bulge of `|v0|·T·16/81`, which IS the legitimate overshoot and is what gets clamped. **THE CONVERGENT BLOCKING FINDING — the commanded-position floor was ON the bottom hard stop.** It shipped as `Homing::HAND_ABS_POS_REV = −0.1` rev, 0.1 rev below the plan's own `[0, 11.1]` bound, justified as *"any margin would make every abort retract infeasible by construction"* — **refuted two lines away in the same function**: the bound is relaxed to `min(FLOOR, start, target)`, so the retract target is an endpoint and is admitted at ANY floor value; re-run at 0.0 every retract still lands on 0.0 and the two undershooting cases take the fallback, which is today's behaviour and is SHORTER. What −0.1 actually bought was permission to command onto the stop the axis homes downward into at −3 rev/s — below the floor the HOST declares (`teensy_bridge_node` rejects a target < 0, `can/odrive.py` clips and warns), so firmware and host disagreed on the legal range. Brute-forced over the honoured branch it admitted troughs to **−0.0999 rev** on the retract, prime AND catch-descent paths, and a PRIME dispatched mid-retract (5.0 rev, −24.6 rev/s) dived to **−0.057 rev — 5 rev BELOW its start**, for a move whose target is the top of the stroke. The ceiling keeps 0.5 rev for exactly the +0.186 rev position-loop undershoot the floor had **zero** allowance for. Floor → `JBOp::HAND_RETRACT_REV = 0.0`; the asymmetry that remains is now deliberate and documented. A 1e-4 rev (3.2 µm) float32 slack had to ship with it — the interior turning point is evaluated in `float`, so a monotone profile can report a trough a few ulps below its OWN endpoint (5.0→0.0 at −7.5 rev/s reads −2.4e-6), and without the slack the tighter bound would have silently disabled the feature it was protecting on the whole retract path. **A SECOND cannot-fit test, added at finalize: DURATION.** Arresting `v0` costs time as well as travel and the two scale differently — duration grows linearly in `|v0|`, excursion as `v0²` — so mid-stroke, where there is room for a big bulge, the excursion clamp alone lets a prelude run arbitrarily long. `catch_coordinator._PRIME_INFLIGHT_S = 1.2 s` is the anti-stutter window (the 2026-07-23 stutter: 5/12 ascents stalled 60-70 ms, reversals to −4 rev/s) and its pinning test's own comment says it exists so *"a Phase-4 duration change cannot outgrow it silently"* — while evaluating only the `v0 = 0` branch. A prime into a live retract (which never stamps `_prime_dispatch_mono`, so it is unsuppressed) at the retract quintic's own peak — start ≈5.04 rev, −24.6 rev/s — solves to **1.206 s**, past the window outright. Raising the host constant needs 1.81 s; chosen instead: bound the FIRMWARE at `smoothMoveMaxDuration()` = the longest **rest-to-rest** move the stroke admits (0.8005 s), derived from constants already in the header, so an honoured prelude can never outlast a profile this firmware **already emitted before the change** — the fallback's own argument, extended from magnitude to duration, keeping every host window valid without moving any of them. Costs the top of the band (20.9 → 20.3 rev/s) against a target settle tail of ≤ 0.25 rev/s. **THE INSTRUMENT WOULD HAVE SCORED A WORKING FIX AS A FAILURE.** The Phase-0 probe separates a `makeSmoothMove` brake from the armed catch descent at 0.5 rev below x3 — sized on the seven observed tosses' brakes (0.206-0.365 rev), **every one of them the settle-from-above case**. A brake seeded from a DOWNWARD velocity dives `0.00778·v0²` rev: past 0.5 rev at just **8.05 rev/s**, honoured out to **3.213 rev**. Synthetic capture at the clamp's reach, shipped probe, old separator restored: `catch_desc` lands on the brake (2.05 s vs the real 3.03 s), `peak` under-reports 9.9602 vs 9.9794, and `dip_below_x3` reads **0.0 rev on a capture that dove 3.21 rev = 102 mm below the stroke end** — a false PASS on the row guarding the 11.1 rev end stop, whose bench row is a hard abort with E-STOP. Re-anchored on the catch region (`x5 + 0.33` rev, which no honoured brake can reach); `deep-brake` is now a permanent gate case, biased deep on purpose because too shallow fails SILENTLY and too deep fails loudly. **The velocity and torque feedforward streams were entirely unvalidated** — the compiled-header driver printed `tr.v.front()` and stopped, so `30.0f*t4 → 3.0f*t4` in `quinticS1` left the full-stroke prime commanding **−354 rev/s** of velocity FF at its final sample with **164/164 green**; a `quinticH1` sign flip (preserving `h1(0)=1`, so `tr.v[0]` still reads `v0`) and any `quinticS2`/`quinticH2` mutation (reaching only `tr.tor`) were equally invisible. Driver now emits every sample; all four mutations caught, each by exactly one test. **And the excursion sweep asserted the code's own acceptance predicate back at itself** (`min(HAND_HOME_ABS_POS_REV, start, target)`) — tautological on the floor side, which is why a 60-case sweep never saw the floor defect; now written against the PHYSICAL limits with two states from shipped profiles added to the grid. REJECTED both of the plan's cannot-fit options with the failure each causes (refusing means a kind-3 does not clobber — the only un-arm mechanism — and refusing via an EMPTY trajectory is worse, since `Teensy_code.ino:472-475` returns BEFORE `packedMsgs.clear()`; braking to the limit needs ~28 000 rev/s² near a target, 280× the declared limit — a figure the first draft over-generalised to every retract and which finalize scoped back to the near-target corner). WITHDRAWN mid-finalize: the `smooth_move_v0_deadband_rps = 6.0` **value**, because the two available measurements disagree — the 5.39 rev/s anchor is a top-park **p99** (max never published) while a stationarity-filtered re-read of the 2026-07-25 traces gives p99 0.134-0.144, max 0.96, suggesting 5.39 is a position-band artefact that swallowed transit samples. Its arithmetic claims were wrong and are fixed (0.2802 rev is the branch's **infimum**, not a bound; the H3.5 argument compares a COMMANDED excursion against a MEASURED-position band); the value is the operator's. Rest-to-rest is bit-identical in every commanded position (the one difference is `−0.0f → +0.0f` on the first velocity sample for δ < 0, same `int16` 0 on the wire); the empty branch is strictly NARROWER, so the un-arm path fires in strictly more cases. **NOT FLASHED** — `Teensy_code.ino` byte-untouched, and the Platform Teensy carries no `FW_VERSION`, so an un-flashed board is indistinguishable from a fixed one at the bench (row H4.0 is the only guard) — *superseded 2026-07-27 by Phase 6: the board now reports `FW_VERSION` on the 0x6E0 reply and run-sheet row **FW-1** detects the un-flashed case directly; this entry's flash is still required, FW-1 is what confirms it.* § CHECK HAND-4, rows H4.0a-c + H4.1-H4.10. Full-suite triple in the entry (xfail unchanged at 3). | [2026-07-27-velocity-continuous-prelude](2026-07-27-velocity-continuous-prelude.md) |
| 2026-07-26 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — catch-reach-degenerate-overshoot Phase 3 | **The trajectory builder stops manufacturing platform motion nobody asked for — and most of the work was stopping the contract that guards it from going silently vacuous.** Operator decision: `planner._CATCH_TILT_THROUGH_RATE_RADPS` `0.07 → 0.0`, so an unopinionated catch is now **reach + quiescent hold** — two segments, rim stationary at ball contact, settle exactly on the commanded target, plan 0.15 s shorter. The framing is load-bearing and is recorded verbatim: platform motion during a catch or throw is **PERMITTED but never MANDATED** — it should be an output of the planner deciding it is optimal, never a constant in the builder. So this is a **default, not a rule**: `tilt_through_rate_radps` is unchanged, the decay path is unchanged, C-CATCH-1 still returns an explicitly-requested rate **verbatim and unbounded** (the seam a future optimising planner uses), and **no assertion anywhere says a catch commands no motion**. THE VACUITY TRAP, which is most of the diff: at a zero default the C-CATCH-1 bound is unreachable two independent and silent ways — `rate == approx(_CATCH_TILT_THROUGH_RATE_RADPS)` degenerates to `0 == 0`, and passing the kwarg takes the deliberately-**unbounded** requested branch — so the whole `test_ccatch1_*` block would have stayed **green while proving nothing**, which is worse than deleting it. Every such test now restores a non-zero **module constant** via `_set_seat_rate(monkeypatch)`, never via the kwarg. **Verified by mutation, not by reading**: both bound halves deleted ⇒ **5 of 7 `ccatch1` tests fail**, the 2 survivors being negative cases by construction (`..._leaves_a_request_alone...`, `..._keeps_the_seat_on_an_on_pose_supersede`); tree restored and re-verified by md5. Same trap closed twice more — the ROS tests capture `_PRE_FIX_SEAT_RATE_RADPS = 0.07` as a **record** not a mirror, and the replay probe's single mirror splits into a *live default* (`0.0`, compared against production) and a *capture record* (`0.07`, pinned to itself), because syncing it would make every pre-2026-07-26 bag score `NOT-REPRODUCED` — the harness reporting history as broken rather than itself as re-baselined. THE RISK, surfaced and deliberately **not** solved in code: the `0.07` existed because *a parked **tilted** rim deflects the ball* (bb-sim geometry finding). The self-toss catch seats **level** so zero costs it nothing (its seat rate was already zero under C-CATCH-1); the **reload** catch seats at **11.08°** — exactly that geometry — and its rim is now stationary at contact. Two facts bound it without removing it: `0.07` has **never** been hardware-validated (its own leg-velocity note was 2× wrong until measured — 14.24 mm/s, not ~7), and until `407154f` the seat aimed off the **plan-frame** tilt, so no bench impression was ever formed on a correctly-aimed one. The reload is the experiment; § Section ZSEAT scores it, ZSEAT-2 setting PASS/ABORT by **binomial sigma** against sitting 4's 15/19 and separating *bounce-out* (the deflection signature) from *missed-arrival* (BB scatter, not evidence). Also caught: § CHECK CCATCH-3 told the operator to ABORT if the commanded tilt is **flat** over the last 0.8 s — flat is now the **PASS**, inverted outright; corrected by a superseded banner listing all four affected rows rather than by rewriting a sibling section, since the runbook is append-only so parallel phases cannot clobber each other. Landed as its own phase because it changes commanded motion on the **shipping reload path**; rollback is the one-line default with C-CATCH-1 already in force to bound whatever replaces it. colcon + relaunch, no flash, no codegen. | [2026-07-26-catch-through-seat-rate-zero](2026-07-26-catch-through-seat-rate-zero.md) |
| 2026-07-25 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — levelling-frame-contract Phase 3 | **The toss now refuses an un-levelled launch — and the gate had to observe the node that APPLIES the correction, because the plan's own step 3 would have passed in exactly the state it exists to refuse.** Phase 0's Table C found the levelling correction is per-PROCESS and observable nowhere: `/gravity_offset` is VOLATILE with one latched push per orchestrator boot and no re-request path, so the `colcon build` + relaunch this very plan mandates empties `trajectory_node`'s correction while the Teensy-persisted `RobotState.levelling_complete` still reads `true`. Verified against ground truth (`orchestrator_node._on_robot_state` assigns it straight from the Teensy), so the plan's step 3 was OVERRIDDEN: `TrajectoryStatus` gains `bool gravity_correction_loaded`, published 5 Hz by the applier; the coordinator stamps every arrival and feeds `platform_levelled = status_fresh AND loaded` into `TossObservations`; `toss_sequencer._step_checking` refuses with `REJECTED_NOT_LEVELLED`, ordered after mocap/streaming and before the hand chain. Justified GEOMETRICALLY, not procedurally — drift is exactly `4·h·sin θ`, i.e. **43 mm** at the ~0.79 m default apex against a **35 mm** cup, so the catch is impossible before the ball is airborne. Three wrong predicates ruled out and pinned: `levelling_complete` (false assurance after a relaunch), `R != I` (a genuinely level machine measures zero tilt and must not be refused), and a sticky cache (no message flips it between a death and its replacement's first status). Transient-local QoS REJECTED — its latch lives in the publisher, so a whole-graph relaunch gains nothing, and a TRANSIENT_LOCAL subscriber silently refuses to connect to a VOLATILE publisher. `_TRAJ_STATUS_STALE_S = 1.0` is probe-sized, not copied: at 5 Hz the sibling 0.5 s is 2.5 periods and the reference bags already breach it once (median 200.0 ms, max **508.5 ms**, four gaps > 0.3 s over 420 s, zero > 1.0 s). Contract **C-LEVEL-1.O** in `ros_ws/docs/levelling_frame.md`. FINALIZE WITHDREW A HYPOTHESIS THE IMPLEMENTER, THE PLAN AND A PRODUCTION COMMENT ALL CARRIED: a half-rebuild does **not** make the topic "go silent" — rclpy re-raises timer-callback exceptions out of `spin()` and `main()` catches only `KeyboardInterrupt`, so **`trajectory_node` EXITS** and `activate` fails at the A2 arm; the operator never reaches a toss, and the documented symptom would have sent them hunting the wrong subsystem. Also added at finalize: non-finite offsets no longer count as loaded (`correction_from_offset` does no validation, so a NaN would have passed CHECKING and surfaced only as a POSITIONING rejection **after** the platform was claimed). DEFERRED with reason, not scope-protection: a dead `trajectory_node` reports `NOT_LEVELLED` rather than naming itself — the toss-only fix would give one node two independent answers to "is trajectory_node alive", and the one-enforcement-point fix (a stamp on `streaming`) changes when a RELOAD refuses. Both Phase-8 toss runbooks swept for the now-mandatory `level` step, and the LG-5 analysis command was validated **two-sided** against four synthetic captures plus two decoy rosout lines. colcon needs **`jugglebot_interfaces` too** — the one phase of this run that does. Hardware deferred — § Section LVLGATE, LG-0..LG-5. Full-suite triple in the entry (xfail unchanged at 3). | [2026-07-25-toss-rejected-not-levelled](2026-07-25-toss-rejected-not-levelled.md) |
| 2026-07-26 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — catch-reach-degenerate-overshoot Phase 2 | **The catch through-seat was aiming at the levelling correction, not at the ball — and the bound that fixes it nearly parked the reload rim at contact.** `build_catch` read `catch_pose[3:5]` as "the receive tilt", a premise that dies the moment a C-LEVEL-1 correction is loaded: a gravity-level catch's commanded tilt IS the correction, so the seat aimed 100% wrongly, the reach swung `+2.32°` opposite a `−0.78°` request, and the platform settled `0.3008°` off gravity and held it through release (16.5 mm of throw-direction error). FIX: the gravity-referenced receive tilt becomes its own `build_catch` argument (level catch ⇒ zero arrival twist **by construction**, not by a threshold), plus contract **C-CATCH-1** (`ros_ws/docs/catch_arrival_contract.md`) bounding every departure a *derived* arrival twist creates — reach excursion AND settle overshoot — to `40/81` of the catch's physical tilt scale, at one enforcement point. The factor is the quintic's own geometry (`min ψ/|φ| = 5/2`, measured), and it **corrects a published repo number**: the sign-reversal threshold is `40/81 = 0.4938`, not `ψ(2/3) = 0.790` — 1.6× too permissive. ⚠️ The finalize review's convergent HIGH: sizing that bound on the *residual* seed→target travel collapsed it on the shipping reload path, where the coordinator re-installs the catch 9–11 times in the last 0.7 s from an already-on-pose seed — arrival rate at **ball contact** `0.070000 → 0.004460 rad/s`, a **15.7× de-rate** that parks the rim at the one instant the seat exists for, with segment count, tests and probe all still reading green. The scale is therefore `max(residual travel, receive-tilt magnitude)`: the displacement-only reading degenerates exactly where its own meaning does, since once the target IS the seed every arrival velocity is "wrong-side" by definition. Reference bag: 5 self-toss reaches `2.32° → 0.0000°`, settle on target; both reload reaches keep the full seat with the aim rotated `4.0997°` and the settle moved `+0.021°`. Two advisory Tier-8b sim points do bind (`0.070000 → 0.059469`) — correct, pinned, outside the gate ring. Separate commit: `peak_leg_*` report-less installs now publish `0.0` instead of the superseded plan's peaks. Hardware sitting deferred — § Section CCATCH. | [2026-07-26-catch-reach-overshoot-fix](2026-07-26-catch-reach-overshoot-fix.md) |
| 2026-07-26 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — catch-reach-degenerate-overshoot Phase 2 (separate deliverable) | **`trajectory/diagnostics` was attributing one plan's predicted leg peaks to another.** The three `peak_leg_*` fields carry the GATE-PREDICTED peaks of the last install holding a `FeasibilityReport`; six install paths have none (`_svc_hold`, `_svc_go_home`, `_install_guard_descent`, `_retry_pending_stop`, `_install_graceful_stop`, the follower's input-loss stop) and left the fields untouched — so a `hold` after a `go_to_pose` published the `go_to_pose`'s numbers under the hold's already-advanced `move_seq`. This is the misreading that cost time on 2026-07-25, when `14.2 / 142.4 / 3950` read identically before and after a catch install and the field was inferred to be cached: it is not cached, it is per-plan for report-carrying installs and stale for the rest, and a bag cannot tell the two apart. FIX: `_install` clears the predicted peaks, so a report-less install publishes `0.0` = "this install carried no prediction" (honest, and distinguishable — a real accepted move always has non-zero peak velocity). The subtler half: `_svc_go_to_pose`'s write moved AFTER its `_install`, because adding the clear alone would have turned a stale-diagnostic bug into a blank-diagnostic one; all four report-carrying write sites were enumerated and checked. Diagnostics-only, no commanded-motion change, no new lock scope. Closes the PREDICTED half of `mvp_bench_runbook.md` open item 7 — the realized/rolling-window half stays open. | 2026-07-26 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — hand-command-continuity Phase 3 | **The hand-catch prime becomes the derived stroke top — and the review found the profile model three of its neighbours were sized against was the wrong one.** `jugglebot_operational.hand_catch_prime_rev` was a hand-maintained `9.858` rev while the quantity its own YAML comment claimed it was — x3, the throw stroke's end AND the catch trajectory's **first sample** — is `9.95940313273228`. It had drifted **0.101403 rev = 3.207 mm** for the life of the constant, and `Trajectory.h`'s `makeSmoothMove` prepends a prelude from wherever the hand physically is to the first sample of whatever is packed, so every "catch from rest at the top" opened with a real **3.2 mm / 76.5 ms** move — the no-op case was never a no-op. Peak commanded **acceleration is UNCHANGED at 100 rev/s²** (the duration formula solves for it), jerk *falls* 1377.5→1370.5, so a taller stroke buys duration, never a harder command. THE REVIEW'S OWN FINDING, outside this phase's charter and converged across two lenses: the `√(a·Δx)` / `2·√(Δ/a)` closed forms three neighbours are sized against are **bang-bang** forms and `makeSmoothMove` is a **quintic** — both a duration (`MIN_THROW_EVENT_DELAY_S`'s "~0.63 s" vs the real **0.758 s**, an upper bound violated by 20 % in the *unsafe* direction) and a velocity (`_THROW_STROKE_VEL_RPS`'s "peak ascent 31.56 rev/s" vs the real **24.63**) were wrong in opposite directions, and this phase propagated both while updating them for the new prime. Root-caused to a **named model** (`hand_stroke.smooth_move_peak_vel_rps` / `_bound_rps`) all four sites now quote, rather than three corrected literals that would leave the fourth site free to re-derive the triangle again. The code guard was **NOT** re-sized — clearing a conservative bound is what a guard should do, and re-sizing to 24.63 would tighten a safety threshold for tidiness — but bench row **H3.7 was recalibrated** `PASS <= 35` → `<= 30` / DEBRIEF 30-40 / ABORT `>= 40`, because at 35 it scored a **26 % overspeed as "on model"** and overspeed on a smooth move is the re-seeded/clobbered-profile signature this whole plan exists to catch. Also fixed: `test_prime_at_the_stroke_top...` pinned the YAML's 4-decimal **rounding artefact**, so a *more precise* value (`9.959403` ⇒ residual under `makeSmoothMove`'s 1e-6 dead-band ⇒ duration 0.0) would have gone RED on an improvement — now a property, two-sided-verified (old 9.858 RED, 4-dp/6-dp/full-float GREEN, 1e-4 rev drift RED, 1e-5 GREEN). `_PRIME_INFLIGHT_S = 1.2` found **unpinned**: sized from the ascent duration but never naming the constant, so a grep-of-the-constant sweep misses it structurally (0.44 s model margin; a future overrun re-dispatches a kind-3 mid-ascent ⇒ the 2026-07-23 stutter). TWO HYPOTHESES WITHDRAWN: the "unexplained stale-YAML codegen run" that left the external BallButler header at `9.858f` was a **reviewer's own mutation test** (`git checkout` cannot restore a delivery target outside this repo — re-running codegen fixed it and proved determinism); and "the 26.5 ms returns to Phase 1's arm-fit budget" is **false** — `PRELUDE_ALLOWANCE_S` is only consulted on the **self-toss** path, where the hand reaches x3 via the throw stroke and the prime never entered, so the saving accrues on the **primed** path to the firmware's own `:533` fit check. Restating `prelude_peak == approx(31.4 → 31.56)` adjudicated **ACCEPT** (tolerance `abs=0.1` unchanged, both inequalities and both latch samples unchanged, one assertion ADDED, zero xfail/skip in the diff). colcon + relaunch, **no flash** (`JBOp::HAND_CATCH_PRIME_REV` is a dead `constexpr` no sketch reads); § CHECK HAND-3 pending. | 2026-07-25 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — catch-reach-degenerate-overshoot Phases 0-1 | **The `+2.32°` excursion from a `−0.78°` target is one plan doing what `build_catch` specifies — and the amplification goes as `1/|tilt|`, so plan 1 makes it WORSE.** All **seven** catch reaches in `2026-07-25_15-17-48` now reproduce offline through the production `planner.build_catch`, seeded only from recorded inputs (FK seed, `levelling.correct_pose` of the wire target, `landing − _PRETILT_EARLY_S` arrival; ONE nuisance scalar fitted, zero-lag residual printed beside it): 5 self-toss pre-tilts at rx rms 0.0049–0.0062° and both reload pre-tilts at 0.0021–0.0025°, 0 FK failures. THE THREE FEATURES, closed-form: the opposite-sign excursion is the quintic ACQUIRING its specified terminal arrival rate (`p = p0 + Δ·ψ + v1·T·φ`, `φ = −4s³+7s⁴−3s⁵`, extremum **−16/81 at s = 2/3** — verified equal to the production `QuinticSegment` to **2.8e-14**; twist term `+2.925°` + displacement term `−0.615°` = `+2.32°` against the echo's `+2.3224°` at the same knot); the 1.385× settle is the through-seat overshoot `1 + 0.5·rate·decay/|tilt|` = **1.384732** (`−1.078408°` vs bag `−1.0784`); the acc/jerk spike is the 0.150 s decay segment (`142.4 / 3950` against the 3.707 s reach's `13.9 / 35` — 10.2× and 113×), landing at the arrival = release − 0.700 s, which is also when the tilt goes flat. ROOT CAUSE: `build_catch` reads `catch_pose[3:5]` as "the receive tilt", true only while the commanded frame IS the gravity frame — with a correction loaded a gravity-level catch arrives as a plan-frame tilt, so the through-seat aims along the correction. **STOP FIRED**: not general to the C2-replan-to-fixed-arrival pattern (`build_timed`/`build_follow`/chase all take a CALLER-supplied twist; only `build_catch` manufactures one), but general to every catch with a correction loaded **including the shipping reload path** — plan re-rated LIVE, item 9 blocked on operator re-prioritisation. Amplification `(16/81)·rate·T/|tilt|` = **3.76** toss vs **0.17** reload, one code path 22× apart — and since C-LEVEL-1 drives the requested tilt toward zero, plan 1 makes this worse, reversing the premise that rated it lowest of four. THE BRIEF'S MANDATORY RE-DERIVATION: single-install reading **CORRECT**, staleness worry **REFUTED** (`move_seq` 47 published `0.0/0.0/0`, 48 published `14.2/142.4/3950` — the field is WRITTEN; the repetition is real lead-invariance). BUT THE EVIDENCE NARRATIVE WAS WRONG THOUGH THE VERDICT WAS RIGHT, and it had already reached a production comment, the probe, the README and the plan: the statistic called *decisive* (plan-end drift 1.9 ms / 21 samples) is **structurally blind to the exact hypothesis it was credited with refuting** — `_plan_and_install_catch` derives its lead from a fixed `arrival_perf`, so a re-plan to the SAME arrival leaves the plan end untouched, and the same bag hands over the counterexample (post-release burst `move_seq` **53→64**, 14 accepted feedbacks, drift **1.02 ms** — LESS than the zero-install window); and there is no status-vs-diagnostics rate advantage, one 5 Hz timer publishes both (1453 each / 290.6 s). Census regated on `move_seq` + **`plan_kind`** (a `HoldPlan` reads `hold` — the direct per-sample detector of the three paths that install by direct assignment) + accepted `target_feedback`. THREE WAYS THE INSTRUMENT WOULD HAVE FAILED A **HEALTHY** CAPTURE, all closed: (1) `_last_rejection` is a lifetime latch (assigned `''` in `__init__` ONLY), so one unrelated early rejection would force `NOT-REPRODUCED`/exit 1 on every later reach — invisible on the reference bag (0 non-empty across 1453 samples); (2) `--self-check` was blind to **2 of 5** mirrored constants — `build_replay` forced its own `tilt_decay_s` into every production call, so mutating `build_catch`'s default 0.15→0.25 left it passing 7/7 (mutation-verified), and nothing compared `_PRETILT_EARLY_S` at all; (3) `/gravity_offset` was read at end-of-bag, not at ingest, so a mid-session re-`level` would score earlier reaches with a later correction. Also corrected: lead-invariance is **NOT** a general property of catch plans (a reload-sized reach runs `82.5→30.1→24.7→15.2→14.0 mm/s` over leads 0.8→8.0 s; the bag's two reload installs published `23.6`/`23.8` vs the toss catches' `14.2`) — losing that qualifier recreates the very inference error the annotation exists to kill; the plan's "phase-swept band brackets the recorded values" was false on both axes and mixed a truncated running max (2416) with a full-plan band (2558–3141); "bit-identical" → identical at the published precision (`3949.7` → `:.0f` → `3950`); and a parenthetical contradicting the landed contract `ros_ws/docs/levelling_frame.md` on the 16 mm was resolved **in favour of the contract** by lever arm (~1 mm is the seat geometry; **16.5 mm** is the throw direction, valid because `hold_after` holds the settle pose through release — confirmed by the bag's own flat `−1.0784°` from −0.50 to +2.00 s). C-CATCH-1's withdrawn stationarity clause had been justified by an "operator principle of 2026-07-26" that exists **nowhere in the repo** — appeal deleted, root cause kept, ratification parked for the operator. `peak_leg_*` staleness ANNOTATED not fixed (the fix reorders `_svc_go_to_pose`'s write to after its `_install`, safety-adjacent; handed over **symbolically** because the first draft's line numbers were already 2 stale and following them would ship a worse defect). `ros_ws/` diff is comment-only, zero executable lines; colcon for parity, no flash, no YAML. | 2026-07-26 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — hand-command-continuity Phases 1-2 | **The catch arm stops clobbering a live throw stroke (C-HAND-1 host half) — and the review found five ways the bench would have believed the wrong thing.** The Platform Teensy holds ONE packed queue and rebuilds it on any kind-0/1/2 command, seeding the replacement prelude from `current_hand_position` with `v=0` — the live velocity is `extern` at `Trajectory.h:47` and **never read**. During a self-toss the kind-1 catch arm landed **8-18 ms after release**, inside the throw's 65 ms decel ramp, so the queue was cleared with the hand at ~120 rev/s: overshoot to **10.17-10.33 rev** (0.775 rev = 24.5 mm from the 11.1 rev guard), yanked **0.34-1.75 rev = 10.7-55.3 mm below** the stroke end, ~300 ms to recover. FIX: a stroke-busy window **derived** from the toss's own announcement (`throw_time + t_dec(|initial_velocity|) + 40 ms`; both inputs already on the wire) enforced at **exactly one point** — `_throw_stroke_gate_ok`, called from `_arm_hand_catch`, the node's only kind-0/1/2 dispatch — so the arm is **deferred, never dropped** (latch left open, dispatch counter untouched). That also makes Phase 2 free: the ack-failure retry re-enters the same gate, turning "further repacks just churn" from an *assumption* (true for a reload, false for a toss) into an enforced precondition. A fixed delay was rejected on `t_dec`'s 2× spread (94.5 ms at 0.55 s vs 47.4 ms at 1.10 s). The model is **shared, not duplicated-and-pinned**: the Phase-5 verdict probe's local `StrokeModel` MOVED into `motion/trajectory/hand_stroke.py` and is imported back, because a pin only catches the quantities it asserts and a `t_dec` divergence would have the bench scoring the fix against a different model than shipped (precedent: `JB_OP_HAND_CATCH_PRIME_REV` is a hand-maintained 9.858 against a 9.9594 stroke top). Contract written down at **`ros_ws/docs/hand_command_continuity.md`** rather than left in a comment a plan-archival would orphan. THE FIVE FINDINGS, two converged across lenses: (1) the closed-window branch promises an **attempt, not a catch** — its fit check budgets the *at-rest* prelude while the hand is mid-stroke (real prelude 0.37-0.76 s), so `:533` may refuse it; the proposed "drop the arm instead" was **REJECTED** after reading the firmware — `:533`'s `return` sits **before** `packedMsgs.clear()` at `:539`, so a refusal leaves the live stroke **intact** and the cost is a lost catch, never a clobbered stroke; (2) `required_arm_lead_s` excludes the **downstream** bridge→Teensy transit (only the upstream leg cancels, via the bridge's own re-stamp) — a 7 ms shortfall at the tightest point against the **115 ms** of slack the gate actually dispatches with, since it fires at the FIRST tick after the window opens, not the last; (3) **`catch/vel_scale` closes the window on its own** — scale **0.45 ⇒ −15 ms** at the 0.55-0.56 s flight with a healthy tracker, inside the shipped `[0.3, 1.5]` range, and that is exactly the corner the optional short-flight check runs in; (4) the **flight-time error is probably not this phase's** — the ball separates at the decel ONSET (`x2` 5.9138 rev) and all seven truncations sit *past* it (6.1965-7.7825), so a null result is expected and must not re-open the queue-clobber question; (5) the deferral is **tick-dependent** (nothing re-enters the gate on a timer), instrumented as new runbook row **H1.7** rather than fixed on an unproven trigger. Also caught: `test_stroke_window_cleared_on_both_latch_edges` exercised only the *disarm* edge — deleting the arm-edge clear left the file green; now drives both, mutation-verified. And the `JB_OP_TOSS_RELEASE_LATENCY_MS == 0.0` pin became a **residual** property, so Phase-5 T0 filling that slot (which makes the window strictly *more* conservative) no longer reds an unrelated module. Two Phase-0 hedges corrected in place: the prelude is never empty (dead-band 1e-6 rev = 0.03 µm; `fmaxf(T, 0.05f)` floor ⇒ 50-76 ms), and `_MIN_EVENT_DELAY_S = 0.3` binds ahead of the Teensy budget ⇒ real windows **395/115 ms**, not 546/208. kind-3 exemption deliberate + pinned (the only un-arm mechanism the Teensy offers). Instrument re-verified two-sided after the model move (25/25 flag + 4-shape accept, fixture byte-identical). colcon + relaunch, **no flash**; hardware validation pending (§ Section HAND, H1.1-H1.7 + H2.1-H2.4). Full-suite triple in the entry (+33 cases, xfail unchanged at 3). | [2026-07-26-hand-command-continuity-arm-gating](2026-07-26-hand-command-continuity-arm-gating.md) |
| 2026-07-26 | resolved | Self-toss anomaly fixes — levelling-frame-contract Phases 1-2 | **One levelling frame lands (C-LEVEL-1) — and the swing the plan promised to remove turns out to be `build_catch`'s, not the frame's.** `trajectory_node` applied the gravity correction on **2 of its 6** external pose-ingest surfaces, so *"level" meant two things inside one node*: the toss's positioning `go_to_pose` parked at plan-frame `rx = 0` while the pre-tilt `catch/dynamic_target` asked for `−0.7788°`, and `build_catch` planned a min-jerk reach **between two frames**. FIX: one shared pure-Python `motion/levelling.py` (sign convention + the non-commutative `R_gravity @ R_target` composition — the two orders differ by `1.268e-3 rad` *entirely in `rz`*, invisible in leg-space telemetry, which is why a second copy is a silent error); normative contract at `ros_ws/docs/levelling_frame.md`; all six E surfaces + `mpc_bridge_node`'s verbatim second copy migrated (per-row grep gate 0/0/0, stored `R` 2 assignment sites). Extraction verified **bit-identical numerically** (36 offset×rotvec pairs incl. rotvec ≈ π, `max|Δ| = 0.000e+00`), not by reading the diff. Enumerated sites rather than one choke point, and the reason is countable: **9 derived seeds + 4 direct plan installs vs 6 external ingests reach the same planner entries**, so a choke point double-applies on every FK seed (`−0.7788°` → `−1.5576°`). Neutral corrected **at use**, never at construction (a stored corrected constant double-applies the instant a new `/gravity_offset` lands — and the operator levels *after* launch). THE FINDING (all three review lenses converged on the label, one on the physics): **the plan's headline claim was false and its bench instrument would have failed a working fix.** `build_catch` specifies a non-zero **arrival twist**, so a reach with `p0 == p1` still collapses to `v1·T·φ(s)`, `φ = −4s³+7s⁴−3s⁵`, `|φ|max = 16/81` at `s = 2/3` ⇒ `peak_above_park = 0.789132° per second of catch lead` **exactly linear, and independent of how well the machine is levelled**. The reference bag's `+2.3204°` pins the lead at **3.70 s**; at that lead this fix moves the physical peak tilt from **+3.0992° to +2.9198°** — 0.18° of a 2.9° swing — while the quantity the runbook gated on **rises** (`+2.3204 → +2.9198`), because pre-fix the park itself sat 0.7788° high and hid part of it. The retired `≥ +3.099° ⇒ ABORT` line fires on a healthy system at any lead ≥ 3.93 s and sat 0.18° from firing at the lead the session actually ran, while the *same bullet* quoted `+0.17…+0.80°` (absolute peaks at 1.2/2.0 s) and the probe printed a third number under the same label — three conventions, one name. What the fix DOES close is the **park**: the platform rested 0.78° off gravity and the two surfaces disagreed. Instrument made two-sided (`--self-check`: post-fix must PASS, pre-fix must FAIL, and a post-fix session contaminated by a long pre-`go_home` ACTIVATE hold must FAIL *with an ambiguity note* — that hold is commanded at `rx ≈ 0` and is CORRECTLY uncorrected, so on a healthy session it can win the longest-plateau vote and read exactly like the pre-fix frame; `--t0` now mandatory in LVL-3), plus sign-aware settle selection (a hard-coded `min()` returns the PARK for a positive-rx correction) and an `implied lead` cross-check that replaces the threshold (pre-fix bag reads 2.94 s against a true 3.70 s). Guard hardened at three mutation-proven blind spots: a new ingest writing `self._follower_target` with no tracked call at all; `from jugglebot.motion.levelling import correct_pose`, which un-instruments both AST manifests AND the behavioural counting fixture at once; and the endpoint-only regression assertion the plan had twice forbidden (now a rate-per-second-of-lead pin at `s = 2/3`, asserted against the pre-fix geometry at the identical lead). Six seeded regressions, each caught, each reverted. SURFACED AND DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED: `build_catch` aims its through-seat residual along `catch_pose[3:5]` — with a correction loaded that IS the correction — settling **0.3008° off gravity-level at ball contact** (closed form `−1.078408°/−0.095775°`; bag `−1.0784/−0.0958`; probe `−1.0775°`, err 0.0009°) ⇒ the 16 mm catch error. Four-branch design fork, changes commanded motion at ball contact on the shipping reload path ⇒ operator decision, pinned as a characterisation test. One reviewer finding REFUTED with the trace (`stow_to_global_mm` is a pure z translation, so the cup-swing compensation's frame is the base frame, not gravity — the ~1.3 mm IS newly introduced, and is worth ~42 mm of release-direction error it removes). colcon + relaunch, no flash; a manual `level` after the relaunch is mandatory until Phase 3. Full-suite triple in the entry (+55 cases, xfail unchanged at 3). | [2026-07-26-levelling-frame-contract](2026-07-26-levelling-frame-contract.md) |
| 2026-07-26 | in-progress | Self-toss anomaly fixes — hand-command-continuity Phase 0 | **The post-throw hand dip measured, and the instrument taught to score a FIXED capture — the half of a probe's charter that had never been exercised.** `tools/probes/hand_stroke_timeline.py` lands (offline, read-only, no production code changed): reproduces **25/25** Context-table rows from the reference trace, worst instant delta **0.4 ms** against a 20 ms tolerance, and characterises the defect across the whole evidence base — **7 self-tosses, all truncated mid-stroke** (`pos_cmd` freezes at 6.1965–7.7825 rev instead of running the decel ramp to `x3` 9.9594), the hand coasting to 10.1653–10.3248 rev with as little as **0.775 rev = 24.5 mm** of headroom to the 11.1 rev guard, then yanked **0.339–1.748 rev = 10.7–55.3 mm BELOW** the stroke end at −17.9…−42.4 rev/s. Mechanism traced in firmware: any kind-0/1/2 `HAND_TRAJ_CMD` re-preludes from the LIVE encoder with a `v=a=0` quintic (`Trajectory.h:242-301`, `current_hand_velocity` extern-and-never-read) then clears the whole queue (`Teensy_code.ino:539`), so a kind-1 catch arm landing in the 65 ms decel ramp replaces it with a rest-to-rest ramp computed from a position the hand is passing at ~120 rev/s — corroborated independently by every seed reading **0.0000 rev** from the live `pos_meas`. Dispatch latency measured **+12.8…+21.9 ms** (bag) / up to **+23.4 ms** (trace, a systematic 1.5–1.7 ms clock-path offset), so `t_release + t_dec` alone expires mid-ramp and Phase 1's 40 ms margin is 1.7× the worst case, not optional slack; `_MAX_ARM_DISPATCHES = 2` honoured (15 dispatches / 10 tosses) — **no separate defect**, and 5 of 10 tosses dispatched only ONCE, so the dip is caused by any mid-stroke command, not by the retry. THE FINDING (all three review lenses converged): **every gated criterion was an artefact of the probe's own control flow, not a measured threshold** — the probe's own model of a *perfect* post-fix capture printed `dip 0.6 mm` / `pullback −0.3 rev/s` while the runbook said PASS was "no dip printed", so an operator would have scored a working fix as FAILED and burned a powered sitting. Root cause: `dip` is peak-minus-bottom, hence non-zero on any healthy overshoot-and-settle — 20.2 mm at the 10.60 rev coast Phase 4 step 2 *charters*, **larger than the smallest real defect** (20.3 mm), so no `dip` threshold can separate the populations. Fixed at the enforcement point, not in four places of prose: new `dip_below_x3` (pre-fix 0.339–1.748 rev vs healthy 0.000–0.001, nothing between), band `<= 0.10` rev = 3.2 mm — the *same* allowance the plan grants for overshoot above `x3`, so symmetric — with the honest margin stated (~100× healthy side, only **3.4×** on the tightest defect); `pullback` demoted to a bounded report (ABORT `< −5.0` rev/s, *conditional on `peak` passing*) because both reviewers' proposed −1.0/−2.0 would falsely abort a healthy settle, measured −0.31/−1.58/−10.03 rev/s at 0.02/0.10/0.64 rev of overshoot. Two more instrument defects: `catch_desc` was ANY downward command, so a `makeSmoothMove` **brake** — which Phase 4 step 3 specifically charters — spoofed it, and since it bounds both the truncation scan and the dip/peak window it put `catch_desc` **567 ms early** and mis-read the end-stop `peak` row (now requires the command to travel 0.5 rev below `x3`, 7.7× clear of the descent / 1.4× clear of the largest brake, with the brake surfaced as `first_neg_cmd`); and the fixed-shape gate ran only at `tof=0.80` where the 0.6 s window binds first, so neither `catch_desc` cap was load-bearing (added a `FLIGHT_TIME_MIN_S=0.55` case — deleting the dip cap now reports **3.83 rev** of dip on a clean capture). All three mutation-verified; the truncation-scan cap recorded as still-unpinned belt-and-braces rather than papered over. HYPOTHESIS WITHDRAWN: the Outcome's "+20 mm, sim from the physical bottom, firmware from the bottom of the effective stroke" is wrong — `Homing::HAND_ABS_POS_REV = −0.1` homes DOWN so encoder zero is **3.16 mm** up, and the 11.1 rev guard lands 0.76 mm *below* the 355 mm top (incoherent at 20 mm: the guard would sit 16 mm PAST the hard stop). Phase 4 would have converted the `[0, 11.1]` rev bound **20 mm = 0.63 rev too permissive** (0.532 rev past the guard) and flashed it; the mapping, the constraint and a "see Confirmation 2" pointer now live *inside* Phase 4 steps 2 and 4. Also: `/rosout` now DETECTED not assumed on both input paths (the runbook's own Recording command records it); the runbook's recorder env corrected to system `python3` **not** the venv (contradicted the recorder's own docstring on the one mandatory capture step); `--json` timestamped so two `CHECK HAND-n` analyses in one sitting no longer clobber; the two conventions this probe deviates from scoped **in the convention documents first**. Deferred: the catch time-origin question, the kind-3 clobber gap, and all bench validation (§ Section HAND carries the 7-row numeric PASS/ABORT table + baseline). No colcon, no relaunch, no flash. Full-suite triple in the entry. | [2026-07-26-hand-stroke-timeline-probe](2026-07-26-hand-stroke-timeline-probe.md) |

| 2026-07-25 | resolved | Self-toss anomaly fixes — fk-convergence-tolerance Phases 0-1 | **A fully converged FK solve could be declared a failure, and `trajectory_node`'s seed path turned that into a hard refusal to stream.** `leg_lengths_to_pose` tested a bare ABSOLUTE residual of 1e-10 mm, but the residual is `\|leg_vector\| - init_leg_length` — a cancellation of two ~650–870 mm operands — so its achievable floor scales as `2.01·eps·L·cond(J)` (bounded every one of 765 Phase 0 poses) and EXCEEDS 1e-10 mm in part of the workspace. Consequence: 26 consecutive `seed FK failed … not streaming until a valid state` ERRORs across 286 ms (2026-07-24 09:08:55), plus 514/28953 `/robot_state` and 37/10453 `/leg_setpoint_echo` vectors of one session unreconstructable offline. The rejected pose was exact to ≤ 4.60e-13 mm = 5.3e-11 of one encoder dead-band; and the failure is spatially CLUSTERED (±1 dead-band around the burst pose fails 96.8 %, vs 0.0 % at z=50/110 flat) so the 10 ms re-seed retried *inside* the failing pocket. FIX: mixed absolute/relative test (`scale` = largest ABSOLUTE leg length, bounded ≥648 mm so it cannot collapse near stow — the risk register's concern, closed structurally) + a stagnation exit gated on a 1e-6 mm absolute physical-irrelevance ceiling (NOT `k·tol`, which would give the MPC plant 12 dead-bands of slack), both in ONE predicate `_fk_accepted`, divergence raise untouched (unsatisfiable sets raise at ≥ 6.5e8× the ceiling). Both mechanisms ship because they fail differently — the finalize sweep found 85 poses worse than the grid the relative term was sized against (max floor 2.18e-10 mm over 13001 poses, 4.1× headroom not 6×). Review caught a REAL regression the implementation introduced: a ±inf target made the derived threshold `inf`, so `inf <= inf` returned the INITIAL GUESS as converged — suppressing the only FK-failure signal (`_fk_fail_count`/`fk_convergence_failure` e-stop unreachable; seed installs a hold at z=0 while parked at 170) — fixed by rejecting a non-finite `scale` (0.126 µs, no allocation). Also fixed: the final Newton step's work was discarded; the RuntimeError quoted the PRE-step residual (up to 3.00× over-stated); the mkdocs criterion table's unescaped in-cell pipes silently dropped every default value. Honest correction: the exhaustion path DID change for the 40 Hz caller (returns where it raised when the last step lands within tol) — kept, but the "bit-for-bit unchanged" comment is withdrawn and the semantics pinned. Full-suite triple in the entry. | [2026-07-25-fk-convergence-tolerance](2026-07-25-fk-convergence-tolerance.md) |

DEFERRED: ERR_TIMEOUT epidemic (own investigation), BB yaw/PV refusals (BB-side), late/scatter misses (ball-held-sensor era). ADDENDUM (post-session): announced-landing timing error measured across both sittings' bags — pooled n=20, bias +0.115 s ±0.017 (tracker-mediated), core σ ≈ 0.035 s, discrete ~+0.31 s outlier mode ONE per sitting (+0.314/+0.320, likely a BB release mode → BB-side review); **BB's 44 ms firmware release-timing constant STAYS untouched** (tracker-latency confound, single-ToF release-vs-flight ambiguity, nothing consumes announced time critically today) — re-tune pre-registered for the ball-held-sensor era (source-timestamped contact, ≥2 ToFs, explain the outlier mode first). | [2026-07-24-phase7-fourth-sitting-openloop-telemetry-ladders.md](2026-07-24-phase7-fourth-sitting-openloop-telemetry-ladders.md) |

| 2026-07-20 | resolved | MVP trajectory bringup — Phase 7 reload: action-driven catch + arm_catch latch (retire CATCH & SHELL) | **RELOAD becomes a self-contained action; the persistent CATCH mode is retired for a catch-armed latch (SHELL retired too; 809.08 cup-plane aim).** The operator questioned whether a bespoke CATCH control mode is needed "just to catch a ball" — it is not. CATCH did exactly one load-bearing thing: flip the `trajectory_node._on_dynamic_target` gate that lets the tracked-ball tilt reach `planner.build_catch`. So "RELOAD supersedes CATCH" = **raise that gate from the `jugglebot/reload` action via a catch-armed latch** (`trajectory/arm_catch`, mirrored on `catch/armed` which gates the hand), not from a mode the operator holds. The catch *mechanics* don't move; only the *trigger*. The action runs **within ACTIVE:TRAJECTORY** (streaming) — it can't copy LEVELLING's stream-suspend because the live catch IS a streaming trajectory — and it now **owns the platform + hand**: on throw-accept it proactively primes the hand to top (9.858 rev) + raises the latch; on CAUGHT it lowers the latch + re-centers (`go_home`, keeps the ball); on any abort it retracts the hand (≈0 rev) + re-centers. Hand-ownership split: the action owns the proactive prime + abort-retract; `catch_coordinator` keeps the reactive fire + gains but is **latch-gated** so it never arms on a stray ball. Phase-1 re-keyed the CATCH motion-transition safety logic (graceful decel-to-rest, `_catch_arrival_perf` freeze reset) to the latch edges via two shared helpers — every graceful stop seeded C2 off `_current_state()`, no discontinuity at the arm/disarm seams. **Ruled out**: the orchestrator-state form (re-introduces the mode-partition duplication for one gate); the LEVELLING stream-suspend pattern (RELOAD needs the live stream up). SHELL was a clean 1:1 GUI alias delete. The Q1 groundwork fix (`bdbd186`) aims BB at the 809.08 mm cup plane (centroid 744.3 + `HAND_CATCH_OFFSET_MM` 64.78), not the centroid — aiming low ate tilt/reach margin. Commits `bdbd186` (Q1) / `4572925` (SHELL) / `caea3ec` (latch) / `790e943` (action) / `5d5f4ae` (CATCH delete) + this docs phase. Scoped gates (`pytest tests/ros tests/motion -q`, 2026-07-21): Phase 1 1634 / Phase 2 1648 / Phase 3 1641 passed. Phase 5 hardware validation is operator-run + deferred. | [2026-07-20-reload-action-catch-latch](2026-07-20-reload-action-catch-latch.md) |

| 2026-07-17 | resolved | MVP trajectory bringup — shaped-planning efficiency | **Shaped `go_to_pose` planning ~1.1 s → ~91 ms (~12×) AND the binding leg-jerk constraint measured 7× more honestly — accuracy and speed were not in tension.** Three composing phases, all software-verified (hardware behaviour unchanged bit-for-bit from the gate; only the planned duration is shorter + planning faster). **1a (`4acaefe`):** numpy-vectorised twin of the shaped feasibility gate at the current n=200 mesh — pure parity refactor (sampler 7.1e-15/5.7e-14/4.5e-13; gate 1.41e-10 at equal mesh), per-pass **185.6 → 7.5 ms**. **1b (`54c1e75`):** `_SHAPED_VALIDATE_SAMPLES` **200 → 1600** (now affordable) — shaped FD-jerk under-measurement **23 % → 3 %** vs a 6400-ref; jerk-bound lateral moves plan **+4.9 % longer** = the intended fix for the silent under-stretch of the "sharp" lean; two plan hedges corrected as empirically FALSE (@6400 is a convergence reference NOT an acceptance oracle; only FD jerk is mesh-monotone, not vel/acc point-samples). **2 (`806e8fb`):** retiming-model duration search (`retime.py`; per-sample `u=1/T` polynomial, cap-free `T_ref`, monotone bisection, **ONE** verify pass through the UNCHANGED gate) behind `JB_TRAJ_RETIME_MODEL` (default ON — **flipped OFF the same evening** after the first hardware A/B: honest durations on lean traverses exceed the tracking envelope, see the retime-model row above) — shaped `build_move` **228.7 → 90.9 ms (2.52×)**, durations uniformly **+2.0 %** over optimum (legacy bisection +1.2–14.7 %), **0 verify rejections** over 54 optimality + 120 fuzz cases; every returned plan still gate-verified (proved from code). **Key insight:** vectorisation decoupled the mesh from the cost (the 1600 bump would have been ~300 ms/pass and unaffordable pre-1a); the model never bypasses the gate (it only *chooses* T). Builder deviations accepted: 1b's `_batched_at` re-anchoring of the parity tests after the clamp interaction; Phase-2 module/API naming drift (`retime.py`/`build_model`/`RetimeModel` vs the plan's `retime_model.py`). Full suite (`pytest tests/ -q`, 2026-07-17): 1a **2860/1 xfailed in 584.45 s**, 1b **2862/1 in 608.38 s**, 2 **2872/1 in 937.31 s**. Deployment: `colcon build --packages-select jugglebot` + relaunch (Python-only; YAML flag regenerated). | [2026-07-17-shaped-planning-efficiency-implemented](2026-07-17-shaped-planning-efficiency-implemented.md) |

| 2026-07-16 | resolved | MVP trajectory bringup — S4 lean A/B: planning latency + boundary vel_ff step | **The lean-0.3 A/B pauses were planning COMPUTE (not motion) and the "sharp tilt" was a boundary vel_ff STEP — both operator hypotheses confirmed, both fixed.** Three-agent investigation (code map + bag forensics on the 929 s `2026-07-16_18-45-29` + offline profiling) on why `--lean-gain 0.3` felt worse than `0.0`. **(1) PAUSES = PLANNING.** Matched A/B at `(1500,5000,20000)` seq111-121 vs 122-132: between-move pause **1.70 → 4.33 s (+2.64 s), ~94 % planning** (settle grew only +0.13 s); offline `build_move` 0.73 → 2.6 s/move. Root chain: a shaped `_ShapedPlan` is barred from the fast vectorized gate (`feasibility.py:403-407` TypeError) → falls to the dense analytic gate, runs 6 `validate()` passes vs 2 (`_SHAPED_REFINE_ITERS=4` bisections), and `compute_jacobian` was **68 % of validate wall time** via `np.cross` numpy generic dispatch (3 Jacobians/sample). **(2) SHARP TILT = boundary vel_ff STEP.** Lean tilt ∝ base accel ⇒ tilt_rate ∝ base jerk, and a rest-to-rest quintic has `j(0)=j(T)=60d/T³ ≠ 0` ⇒ a **~70–182 mm/s (1.0–2.6 rev/s)** leg vel_ff step at every shaped-move boundary (**0.00** unshaped); bag-corroborated (+43 % peak iq 5.93→8.48 A at matched limits, 1.52× first-60 ms vel). `validate()` is structurally blind (never differences across the hold→move seam) — the trajectory-layer analogue of the K5 twist-discontinuity class; the "disconnected" feel is structural (lean is a self-cancelling pulse peaking at ~0.211 T, upright at every stop). **(3) SAFETY** at `(1500,5000,40000)`: peak dev 0.359 rev = 36 % of the 1.0 rev guard, peak leg vel 285 mm/s = 54 % of clamp cap, 0 faults / 143 moves; jerk is the binding constraint. **FIXES (this commit):** `ik_solver.compute_jacobian` vectorized to a component-form cross (numerically identical — bit-equal to np.cross on the same operands, ≤2.84e-14 ≈1 ulp vs the old per-leg loop; unshaped build_move 736→234 ms, shaped 2680→1183 ms); `feasibility.py` default sampling 200→**80** for unshaped with `_VALIDATE_JERK_MARGIN=1.05` covering the ≤3.7 % FD under-measurement on the BINDING jerk constraint (adopting `validate_follow`'s pre-existing idiom), shaped floored at `_SHAPED_VALIDATE_SAMPLES=200` (its FD jerk under-converges — window-edge curvature post-window); `shaping.py` multiplies the ENTIRE lean contribution (tilt + xy shift + all derivatives) by a **C2 plateau window** `w(s)=S(s/EDGE)·S((1−s)/EDGE)`, EDGE=0.15, applied by full product rule in `state_at` (window-then-cap) — self-consistency preserved. **Net offline (x+150): unshaped ~0.73→~0.10 s (~7×), shaped ~2.6→~1.2 s (~2.3×); boundary vel_ff step 0.00 mm/s at both ends for all tiers.** Low-pass FF REJECTED (breaks pose/twist/accel self-consistency, the 2026-05-08 class); septic base REJECTED (blast radius); fewer refine iters REJECTED (`_stretch_factor` assumes 1/Tⁿ vs lean's 1/T³ → durations balloon 1.04→6.9 s). **Status in-progress — operator must re-run the lean A/B (colcon build + relaunch):** expect pauses ~4.3 → ~2 s (settle-dominated), onset iq spikes gone; the open A/B question is whether lean *helps* realized smoothness at matched limits (pre-fix it WORSENED peak iq). Remaining for later: vectorized shaped gate (~100× endgame), analytic shaped stretch seed, carry-tilt-across-moves (the true fluidity fix). | 2026-07-16 | resolved | MVP trajectory bringup — S4 limit ramp: MAX_DEVIATION guard + vel_limit headroom | **The MAX_DEVIATION guard latched on legitimate velocity-loop lag, not runaway — guard raised 0.5→1.0 rev, leg vel_limit 4.0→6.0 rev/s, per-leg latch attribution wired.** The S4 limit ramp PASSed at 100/660/10500 then latched the Teensy guard three times at vel=200 mm/s (bags `2026-07-16_13-17-58` @528.371 s + @621.567 s; `2026-07-16_13-34-58` @72.502 s), always leg 1 (0-indexed) first, frozen trips −0.5196/−0.5603/−0.5515 rev, always at move onset of the same asymmetric sweep; jerk 8000→10500 made no difference. Root cause (six-agent parallel investigation + adversarial cross-check): **legitimate velocity-loop lag under coordinated-move load, NOT runaway.** The guard watches the RAW streamed 40 Hz knot u0 vs encoder PRE lead-clamp (`fault_machine.cpp:367-371`), so it counts exactly the command-space lag the lead clamp (`enc ± MAX_LEAD 0.10`) deliberately tolerates. Peak \|dev\| is superlinear in realized leg velocity (v^1.19 all-legs / v^1.39 leg 1: 0.079 rev @35-50 mm/s → 0.544 @170-200, where all 6 move records belong to the 3 latch events), implied lag τ grows 112→208 ms (NOT a fixed 6-15 ms transport delay); deficit-integral closes it (−0.594 rev computed vs −0.516 accumulated at the last 10 Hz pre-trip sample; frozen snapshot −0.5196). Drives never railed: peak iq_setpoint 8.73 A of 10 A, peak encoder vel 2.23 of 4.0 rev/s, active_errors 0 throughout. H1 transport-delay / H2 vel_limit-saturation / H4 lead-clamp-limit-cycle / H5 current-rail / H6 stroke-clamp / H7 command-discontinuity all REFUTED; H3 (accel-phase velocity-loop lag) is root. Withdrawn mid-investigation: the vel_limit-saturation framing AND the 'encoder capped at vel_limit under clamp' corollary (setpoint saturates, encoder does not). Operator-approved response (all four): **(1)** `MAX_DEVIATION_REV` 0.5→1.0 rev (firmware, `canbridge_config.h`; FW_VERSION 1→2 human-facing only — sole consumer is the boot Serial banner, nothing runtime); **(2)** ODrive leg `vel_limit` 4.0→6.0 rev/s (config→codegen→runtime CAN push at `teensy_bridge_node._run_configure`:3035, fires after every /home /configure /activate) — un-saturates Kp·MAX_LEAD=40×0.10=4.0 ≤ 6.0 so vel_ff regains catch-up authority (was 4.0=vel_limit exactly); bench envelope ~48 rev/s, overspeed guard 16.5 rev/s unchanged; **(3)** per-leg latch attribution from the firmware's frozen `max_dev_leg` snapshot → bridge log + `/link_status guard_fault_leg` + GUI minimap tooltip `(MAX_DEVIATION, leg N)` (zero Python re-thresholding — auto-tracks the firmware threshold); **(4)** runbook ABORT rules recalibrated (0.1 rev at HOLDS only; moves may run to ~0.6 rev but must collapse at arrival). Raising the guard adds ZERO physical excursion for command-side faults (lead + stroke clamp + ODrive clip bound the executed command); accepted cost = encoder-side-runaway detect distance 35→70 mm. `MAX_LEAD` stays 0.10. **Deployment is two independent halves, neither on hardware yet:** (A) guard needs a Teensy REFLASH (`pio run -e teensy41 -t upload`; built clean, Flash 3.2%/RAM 27.2%); (B) vel_limit needs `colcon build --packages-select jugglebot` THEN relaunch (a stale install keeps pushing 4.0). Next chapter = accel FF, sized on a MEASURED coordinated-move inertia (1-3e-3+ kg·m²), NOT the bench single-leg J_eff≈3e-4. Scoped `python -m pytest tests/motion/ tests/teensy_link/ tests/firmware/ -q` (2026-07-16): **1018 passed in 110.58 s**; firmware `pio run -e teensy41` SUCCESS; full suite (`pytest tests/ -q`, 2026-07-16): **2811 passed / 1 xfailed in 772.17 s**. **EVENING ADDENDUM: both halves VALIDATED on hardware — bag 17-38-15 ramped vel 100→200→280 mm/s, 22 moves, ZERO latches (the battery that latched 3× at vel=200 that morning). Envelope then opened further: vel_limit 6→12 rev/s, set_limits ceilings 280/4000→5000/5000 (jerk 200000 unchanged; ceilings now administrative). | 2026-07-15 | resolved | Leg feedforward — Kt CONFIRMED + SHIPPED (0.0570), tff channel verified | **First Kt measurement: 0.0577 ± 0.0012 Nm/A — NEITHER candidate** (ODrive's 0.0551 excluded +2.1σ low, the historical 0.0624 excluded −3.8σ high — the stiction-bias prediction confirmed). Recovered from the operator's four-mass friction-cancelling traverse session (0.5/1.0/1.5/2.25 kg) against ACTUAL masses — the harness had mislabelled every run "1.00 kg" (`--hold-mass` is a Mode-2 flag, silently ignored by Mode 1); fit R²=0.99909, intercept ≈0. PROVISIONAL: masses operator-recalled, and stiction-confounded hold-currents pull mildly toward ~0.062 — the weighed-mass confirmation run settles it; YAML keeps 0.0624 until then (updating it auto-corrects the FF wire scale — the double-correction-proof design). **Bonus: friction GROWS with load** (half-diff 1.16→1.51 A over 0.5→2.25 kg; cancels in the fit; unmodelled in Stribeck). **The torque_ff channel is SETTLED**: positive wire tff = extension torque, production chain correct as built, **NO negation** (3 inverted-rig runs, ≥10σ); decay-corrected gain ≈18.2 A/Nm vs 18.14 expected (±15–20%). The 20:55 "MUST NEGATE / −21 A/Nm" is dead: **drift-aliasing** — a monotonic rung ladder at fixed 3.2 s cadence makes time and torque perfectly collinear (slope_alias = 160×drift; that run's hand-contamination drift was 2× what's needed), injection path byte-identical across harness versions. **The static-hold Mode 2's steady-state signal is EXACTLY ZERO by construction** (the integrator re-pins total iq at the load value) — what the runs measured was the transient at ~1.3 s of decay (τ_locked ≈ 8–10 s); the orchestrator's traverse-A/B replacement was ALSO refuted in review (during-motion τ = vel_gain/vel_int = 0.625 s absorbs ~83%) → **Mode 2 rebuilt as EDGE-CAPTURE** (settle gate → 15 s pre-soak → ±X square wave inside the friction band → matched-filter edges → decay-corrected jump/(2X); drift tested BEFORE the null test; tri-state channel_live). **The friction-band load-transfer transient** poisons the first ~7 s of any static hold: amplitude ≈ τ_c (1.2–1.4 A, LOAD-INDEPENDENT), τ ≈ 3.2 s — 15 s pre-soak kills it; no rung re-ordering does (0/5040 permutations). **Mode-1 failure chain, one root cause**: starved steady gate (accel sub-gate rejected ~95% of steady samples) → mass "FAILED" → park ran a bring-up helper ARMED AND SILENT (hard 0.3 s sleep) → guaranteed MPC_STALE latch → re-arm tripped → "CANNOT PARK". All fixed + 135 kt_lib tests: parse-time mode/flag refusals, reworked steady selector (n 70–168 on the real CSVs with the landed gate; the recovery-analysis gate kept 47–159), keep-alive flat-knot pump across armed gaps, full stream-then-arm park re-arm (+ armed fast path that never drops the load), abort reasons survive NaN snapshots, **manifests persist per-traverse immediately** (an abort can no longer cost the data), REQUIRED `--rig-orientation` (the inverted rig had silently wrong-way sign bookkeeping), conditioning floor downgraded to a warning (the 0.5 kg point empirically refuted it). **Firmware ingest clamp BUILT (review: SHIP, zero findings)**: |torque_ff| ≤ 0.25 wire-Nm at UDP ingest (clamp-don't-reject), per-leg torque_clamp_mask on heartbeat flags bits 8–13 (free bits — no size change, no version bump), mirrored on /link_status; three-layer chain pump 0.1325 → firmware 0.25 → ODrive 10 A; both pio envs build, codegen idempotent, 210 firmware/parser/bridge tests — **DORMANT UNTIL FLASHED**. SAME-DAY CONFIRMATION (weighed masses ±10 g, 2 reps, flashed): harness's own fit **Kt = 0.0564 ± 0.0011** (R²=0.99999) → **pooled 0.0570 ± 0.0008 SHIPPED to YAML** (0.0624 excluded 6.6σ; ODrive's 0.0551 at +2.2σ — the nameplate was nearly right); wire scale auto-corrected **0.8835 → 0.96725** with pump/tests/docs rippled in lockstep. Edge-capture's first hardware contact found one more design bug — the amplitude bound checked the LEVEL but a square wave toggles a SWING of 2X (±0.035 swing = 0.070 ≈ 1.5× the band → chaos; ±0.020 swing = 0.040 → 12% low) AND the pool gated globally, refusing a t=147 measurement over one bad amplitude → **fixed** (swing-safe defaults ±0.005/0.010/0.015; per-amplitude data-quality exclusion, t-stat stays pooled so a NULL still reads DEAD). Replayed verdict: **trustworthy, +18.351 ± 0.224 A/Nm (t=82) vs 18.14 expected — channel verified end-to-end**, and the bench drive's flashed torque_constant verified live (implied 0.0545 ± 0.0007, 1σ from 0.055133). **Gravity-FF arming session is next with nothing in front of it.** | [2026-07-15-kt-first-measurement-and-tff-channel](2026-07-15-kt-first-measurement-and-tff-channel.md) |
| 2026-07-14 | in-progress | Leg feedforward — Kt + gravity FF | **The Kt mismatch is a STEALTH GAIN CHANGE, not a stale constant — reconciled on the wire; gravity feedforward re-applied (DEFAULT OFF).** Follow-on to the 2026-07-13 gain-hunt closure. **(1)** On ODrive 0.6.x the velocity controller outputs TORQUE and the drive computes `Iq = torque/torque_constant`, so the loop's authority is `vel_gain/torque_constant` — meaning **"just set torque_constant to the measured Kt" would silently DETUNE the shipping, bench-validated velocity loop by −11.7 %** with no error and no telemetry. That is the one action that must never happen. Confirmed three ways: operator + the version-pinned 0.6.11 API ref (`vel_gain : [Nm/(rev/s)]`) + **the robot's own stability** (amps-semantics would need 30.5 rev/s of velocity error to explain an observed 6.09 A peak against a 4.0 rev/s vel_limit, and would put ω_v at 1.26 Hz — BELOW the 6.37 Hz position loop, an inverted cascade that rings unconditionally). **Resolution: do NOT touch the drives.** The YAML now carries BOTH constants (`motor_kt_nm_per_a` = physical truth, `motor_kt_odrive_config_nm_per_a` = what is flashed) and codegen derives `ODRIVE_LEG_TORQUE_WIRE_SCALE = 0.8835`; pre-scaling torque_ff on the wire makes the DELIVERED CURRENT correct at zero risk to the loop, and is **double-correction-proof** (flash the true Kt later, update the mirror, the scale auto-becomes 1.0). If it IS ever changed it must land ATOMICALLY on three surfaces (YAML gains, FLASHED gains, FLASHED torque_constant) — homing/activate run before configure and use the flashed values, so two-of-three is a silent ±13 % loop change. **(2)** The same division **falsified yesterday's cascade table**: ω_v = vel_gain/(2πJ) omitted it. Corrected **ω_v 22.8 Hz / ratio 3.58** (was 20.2/3.17) — healthier, not worse — and **ω_v is INVARIANT to the true Kt** (J_eff scales with Kt, the measured slope as 1/Kt), so it survives however Kt lands. **(3) Kt itself is NOT settled**: 0.055133 is ODrive's uncalibrated nameplate `8.27/Kv` to 15 s.f. with zero empirical backing (and the datasheet CANNOT adjudicate — 3.86 Nm/70 A = 0.05514 = the formula, circularly), but 0.0624 has **two** problems: a **lever-arm BUG fixed this session** (`single_leg_test.py:841` used the STANDARD leg's 11.221 mm spool radius on the BENCH leg's 11.391 mm ⇒ Kt came out 1.51 % LOW ⇒ corrected **0.0633**, i.e. FURTHER from ODrive's value) and an **unquantified stiction bias** (a position-hold fit; τ_c ≈ 1.1 A supports part of a static load ⇒ Kt biased HIGH). **Both 2026-07-13 plant-ID conclusions survive at either Kt** — τ_c is Kt-INVARIANT (raw regression coefficient in amps; the 2026-04-27 corroboration is amps-vs-amps), and the J_eff ≈ 1× J_rotor refutation of "~10× rotor" holds by an order of magnitude either way. **(4) Gravity FF re-applied, DEFAULT OFF** (`motion/torque_ff.py`): SetpointPump is the single wire enforcement point — **clamp → Kt scale → ramp**. The clamp (0.15 Nm) is load-bearing because **there is NO other clamp at any layer** (firmware saturates only at int16 ≈ 59 A of demand) and the ODrive ADDS input_torque BEFORE the torque limit, so a large FF saturates the current clamp, **the position loop loses ALL authority**, and the leg runs open-loop until MAX_DEVIATION E-STOPs it mid-motion. The **ramp-in** exists because `vel_integrator_limit` is genuinely **inf** and the integrator has already wound up to carry gravity — a step FF would command ~2× gravity until it unwinds. Platform-inertia term gated OFF: the firmware HOLDS torque_ff **undecayed** through a stale-link window (`leg_interp.cpp:366` sits after the velocity-decay block), so an accel-proportional FF would keep pushing at full magnitude while commanded velocity decays — gravity, being static, is correct to hold. **SIGN VERIFIED BY INDEPENDENT ENERGY BALANCE** (not by reading the code): FF virtual work per 1 mm platform lift = **mgh exactly, ratio 1.00000 at four poses** incl. tilted + extreme; all six torques positive; magnitudes land in the historic 0.013–0.041 Nm/leg band; **mutation-testing the sign fails 11 tests**. Flag-OFF proven inert (even a NaN torque_Nm neither leaks nor rejects the frame). **The iq SIGN-FRAME TRAP**: `can_buses.cpp:92` stores iq RAW while pos/vel go through `leg_sign` — a code-read says extending torque should read NEGATIVE iq, which would make the plant-ID sign-broken. **The data refutes it** (corr(iq,accel)=+0.39, corr(iq,sign v)=+0.81; +36 rev/s² draws +0.89 A) — the drive's own direction calibration inverts it back. Per-axis calibration is a property of each DRIVE, not the firmware — re-verify before porting to the robot's legs. **Kt experiment ready** (`kt_bench_test.py`): constant-velocity up/down traverse under known masses, where `(iq_up+iq_down)/2 = τ_g/Kt` **cancels friction EXACTLY** and the half-difference yields a free independent friction measurement; 1.0–3.0 kg at 0.6 rev/s; resolves 0.0551-vs-0.0624 at ~7.5σ (cannot resolve 0.0624-vs-0.0637, and says so); **requires BENCH_SYSID_BUILD** (DIAGNOSTIC gates on iq_setpoint not iq_measured, so stock v3 gives 2–3 samples per traverse) and hard-refuses below 150 Hz telemetry. Run `--mode torque_ff_check` FIRST — it settles the torque_ff sign through `leg_sign` empirically. Suite `pytest tests/ -q` (2026-07-14): **2701 passed, 1 xfailed** (+ the known load-flaky alloc test, which passes isolated). No firmware change, no gain change, no ODrive re-flash. **ADVERSARIAL REVIEW COMPLETED same day (after the spend limit lifted): 4/4 lenses FIX_FIRST, 15 findings (6 HIGH), all 14 software fixes landed same-session** — the recovery-step hole (the FF ramp missed the guard-latch → /recover path, the most common integrator-rewind event → `SetpointPump.restart_torque_ramp()` wired into both /recover clear paths), the ungated THIRD producer (HardwarePlant read NO config flags; enabling the master switch would have armed the MPC path's accel-proportional inertia term → per-term skip flags threaded through the whole chain + pinned), the **missing Newton–Euler transport moment** in `compute_inertia_wrench` (`r_com × m·a_com` — a 4 m/s² vertical throw demanded ~0.33 N·m of moment and got ZERO; pre-existing since March, fixed + pinned by independent rotational virtual work), a 2π-mutation test gap closed, a clamp ceiling added (0.30 Nm — the ODrive adds input_torque BEFORE its torque limit), the TRAJECTORY producer's singular/ill-conditioned-Jacobian FF now holds-then-decays instead of stepping to zero (the doubly-gated-off MPC producer still zero-steps — known-open) (with a 0.5 Nm physical sanity bound, since solve() returns huge FINITE forces on ill-conditioned J), and the Kt harness rebuilt (see below). One LOW deferred to the firmware sitting (stroke-clamp FF pulses). **FIRST HARDWARE CONTACT validated the review, not the feature**: the operator's messy torque_ff_check run (0.8 kg, odd angle, hand support) produced R²=−0.099 yet the harness confidently printed 'MUST NEGATE / MISMATCH −63%' — review finding #1 demonstrated live. ~~De-noised reading~~ **⚠️ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-15**: the "in-band |slope| 20–22.5 A/Nm, ~17% high" and the "negative bench sign" were BOTH the same **drift-alias + hand-contamination** artifact — three clean 2026-07-15 runs settle the channel at **positive wire tff = extension, gain ≈18.2 vs 18.14 expected, ≥10σ, matching the production chain (NO negation)**; the 1/21.3=0.047 torque_constant speculation is resolved (drive consistent with 0.055133) — see the 2026-07-15 row. Still true from this row: the ±0.10 rungs saturated/re-absorbed exactly as the review predicted — per-drive ODrive direction calibration differs bench-vs-platform, so **a bench sign result must NEVER be copied to the platform; the platform-validated production sign stands**. Harness now refuses verdicts below R²≥0.9 / t≥3, defaults its ladder inside the friction band (±0.035 Nm max), actually parks on abort paths, shapes the traverse start, and frames bench-sign results with the per-drive context. | [2026-07-14-kt-reconciliation-and-gravity-ff](2026-07-14-kt-reconciliation-and-gravity-ff.md) |
| 2026-07-13 | resolved | S4 gain-retune — CLOSED | **The leg gain hunt ends — a rev/mm units bug manufactured the "accuracy knee", and the plant-ID says feedforward, not feedback, is the lever.** The operator ran the missing sustained S4 datapoint, said he was **unconvinced the gain search had found the right answer**, and asked to question the methodology instead of running more points. He was right. Three findings, all from data **already on disk** (no new hardware): **(1)** `bench_leg_sysid.py:2548` printed `err_rms * 1e3` under an `mm` label, but `err_rms` is in **REVOLUTIONS** — that is milli-revs, **~14× too large** (the bench leg is 71.5708 mm/rev). The "10.3 → 6.9 mm accuracy knee at pos 70" is really **0.74 → 0.49 mm**; and against the operator's spec — **±1 mm judged AT THE CATCH/THROW INSTANT** — production `40/0.20/0.32` measures **0.054 mm median / 0.192 mm worst (0/21 strokes over 1 mm), ~5× inside**, so the entire accuracy case for raising pos_gain evaporates. Four rounds of adversarial verification missed it because every check was *internal to the number* — nobody asked whether **10 mm RMS was plausible on a 280 mm-stroke leg**. **(2)** Plant-ID by torque-balance regression (`2πJ·a = Kt·iq − τ_c·sgn(v) − b·v`) on the same CSVs: **J_eff = 0.7–1.2 × J_rotor** (5 fits; two highest-R² both 0.97×) — **refuting the unsourced "J_eff ~10× rotor"** guess carried since 2026-04-27 by an order of magnitude; τ_c = 0.88–1.22 A brackets the 2026-04-27 bench's 1.094 A. Implied production cascade **ω_v 22.8 Hz / ω_p 6.4 Hz, ratio 3.58 — already healthy** (scatter across the 5 fits: ω_v 17.7–28.8 Hz, ratio 2.78–4.53). NOTE the cascade must include the ODrive's `torque_constant` division (vel_gain is Nm/(rev/s), and Iq = torque/torque_constant using the drive's CONFIGURED 0.0551, not the true Kt) — so the shipping velocity loop runs ~13% stiffer than its nominal vel_gain implies. ω_v is INVARIANT to the true Kt, so this survives the open Kt question; the ladder's `vel_gain ∝ √pos_gain` rule holds that ratio *constant*, so climbing it never improved damping, it only slid both loops toward the resonance (which is what the operator's ear heard at pos 110). **(3)** Error budget (contributions of correlated regressors — they do NOT sum): against 0.83 mm pooled RMS, **the delay-shaped term alone contributes 0.57 mm and is gain-independent** (largely the metric's own sample-and-hold — `cmd_u0` is the knot at the START of the segment the firmware interpolates THROUGH), 0.17 mm inertial + 0.15 mm friction (the two switched-off FF terms), 0.44 mm noise floor. **Reframe:** Jugglebot's reference is known in advance with exact derivatives (`plan.state_at` → pose/twist/accel; exact velocity + acceleration IK already computed), so **feedforward follows the trajectory and feedback only cleans up model error** — the ladder used the stability-limited lever to chase an accuracy target the accuracy-limited lever had already met. `vel_ff` (a feedforward) was the single biggest historical win (lag 31→6 ms) and is *why* the error is already sub-mm. **SpaceMouse** is architecturally identical to a ball-tracked catch (same 40 Hz quintic replan path) so it is not off-path — but it is a **worst-case soak** (chase clamp rides 85 % of limits indefinitely) and its harshness has a **non-gain cause**: a fresh quintic every 25 ms restarts *jerk* at 40 Hz, and the firmware's *cubic* Hermite makes executed accel step at every knot ⇒ pass/fail robustness screen, **never a gain selector**. **Outcome: production `40/0.20/0.32` STANDS**; Stage-2 candidate `70/0.35/0.56` + `MAX_LEAD 0.057` **RETRACTED** (MAX_LEAD stays 0.10, already shipped — no firmware change); the gain ladder is **demoted to a stability-envelope probe**; the ±1 mm spec becomes the **stopping rule the methodology never had**; the loaded S4 replay is **reframed as a v3-firmware regression test at production gains**. Effort moves to **feedforward** — `torque_ff` is plumbed end-to-end to the ODrive and carrying zeros (one line: `setpoint_pump.py:263`), leg accel is already on the wire as `acc_mm_s2`, and J_eff is now measured. **Open/blocking:** the **gravity+inertia FF appears to have been dropped by ACCIDENT** (the cutover claimed `:5557` carries `torque_Nm = zeros`; `hardware_plant.py:488` publishes it live — though note MPC is dormant on this branch, and the magnitude 0.013–0.041 Nm/leg is cited from a 2026-05-08 archived plan, not re-measured) and a **13 % Kt mismatch** (ODrive 0.0551 vs measured 0.0624) is latent under any torque FF. The unloaded leg **never reproduced the S4 ~6 Hz limit cycle** in any regime (ladder, sustained tracking, or deliberate clamp-riding at 46 %) — arrive-and-settle stimuli are the wrong excitation class; given the v2→v3 clamp change (vel_ff kept, MAX_LEAD 0.10) the signature may already be fixed on the robot, so **the loaded robot replay decides**. The first ladder "winner" **110/0.50/0.72 was OVERTURNED** — the operator heard/saw motion-excited HF vibration the quiescent + settle instruments structurally miss; the chirp peak/DC then measured effective **ζ≈0.49–0.52 at the 17.5 Hz crossover** (step-fits overstate damping — ramp-limited steps never excite the resonance), 3.7× baseline 20–45 Hz iq energy, ζ non-reproducible (0.78→0.66–0.68), iq railing 1.26 % at 4.4 rev/s. Withdrawn mid-arc by the verify stage: the "18:03 mode-all ran at winner gains" misread (code-confirmed BASELINE at bench_leg_sysid.py:1800) and the chirp "~15 Hz corner" (friction describing-function, not a loop corner; chirp gain ~0.5 at 0.02 rev = real Coulomb friction, 2026-04-27 tau_c=1.094 A corroborated). Round-4 strokes battery + vel_int de-confound: the sustained-stroke triple `MAX_DEVIATION` latch was a **harness sizing bug** (2A swings timed for A → 7.6 rev/s, infeasible at any gains; fixed db05670 + a series-level velocity gate closing the class); baseline HF onsets on 99 ms strokes are commanded-content **false positives** (velHF FALLS with gain), real vibration GROWS with gain on long strokes (iqHF to 5.6× at pos 90). Bench envelope: **pos 40 clean / 70 accuracy knee (mean errRMS 10.3→6.9 mm, −33 %) / 90 aggressive edge / 110+ out** [**2026-07-13: those errRMS values are milli-REVS, not mm — really 0.74→0.49 mm; the "accuracy knee" is retracted, the stability envelope stands**]; pos 130 dead at all vel_int (the boundary itself).
