| 2026-07-12 | resolved-with-open-items | GUI + catching cone | **Cone "only syncs after a colcon build" — zombie AND build-correlation hypotheses BOTH REFUTED by bag ground truth; a REAL but LATENT UDP co-bind hazard found; the morning failures point at CONE MCU RESTARTS.** Follow-up to [[2026-07-12-gui-cone-catch-correlation-regression]]. Operator report: *"cone syncs perfectly immediately after a colcon build, but subsequent launches don't sync — zombie ROS2 processes?"* **No zombies** (live forensics: only the systemd GUI daemon + the `ros2` CLI daemon). **No build-correlation**: decoding `/cone/heartbeat` from EVERY bag of the day (**17 sessions**) shows **14/17 `connected=True` AND `time_synced=True` FROM THE FIRST PUBLISHED HEARTBEAT** — the `19-16-18` (pre-rebuild) / `19-17-31` (post-rebuild) / `19-19-12` (next launch, the one the hypothesis says must FAIL) triple is identical (**a colcon boundary with NO signature change**), and three further launches 45 min–3 h later with no build between all sync from their first HB. The operator's model was a CORRECT lesson from finding (1) — the `catch_correlation_node` orphan GENUINELY needed a rebuild (`ros2 launch` runs the INSTALLED copy) — **over-generalised from launch-graph membership to device time-sync**; the defence is to restate the *mechanism*, not the correlation, when reusing a prior lesson. **FINDING 1 — a real structural hazard (HIGH confidence, read straight off the code):** `teensy_bridge_node` and the parallel session's **bench system-ID harness** each construct a `TeensyLinkClient` binding the SAME can-bridge ports (`0.0.0.0:5005` stream + `:5006` RPC) with `SO_REUSEADDR` (`controller/teensy_link/client.py:174-180`); the second bind succeeds SILENTLY and the kernel resolves each unicast datagram to **exactly ONE** socket (no `SO_REUSEPORT`, no load-balancing) ⇒ **one process gets EVERYTHING, the other gets NOTHING**, winner decided at bind time ⇒ since the bench harness **does not subscribe `CONE_FRAME`**, a healthy cone would present as a disconnected one with **no error, no warning, no log line on either side**. **FINDING 2 — that hazard did NOT cause this incident (attribution DOWNGRADED to LOW / candidate-only, NOT supported by the timing artifacts).** The draft blamed the two dead morning sessions (`10-37-19`, `10-42-51`: `connected=False` for **0/882 and 0/1606** heartbeats) on the co-bind because bench runs "bracketed" them — but **a bracket is ADJACENCY, and the mechanism needs CONCURRENCY**: bench run 1 ran 10:36:57→**10:37:00** and the dead bag's first message is **10:37:23** (23 s AFTER it exited); the second dead bag's last message is **10:45:38** and bench run 2 starts **10:46:00** (22 s later). Across **all 17 bags × all 22 `bench_sysid_*` runs of the day there is NOT ONE overlapping pair** — the co-bind event never occurred. Both morning bench runs are **3-second aborted runs** (manifest-only, `stages: {}`) and could not have starved an 89 s / 163 s session even if they HAD overlapped. The signature also **flips mid-session** in three other bags (impossible for an all-or-nothing bind race) and **appears in the `cone_test_*` bags of 2026-05-24 — six weeks BEFORE the bench harness existed** (`f218acf`, 2026-07-11; `TeensyLinkClient` itself 2026-06-02). **FINDING 3 — the LEADING explanation is cone-side power/wiring intermittency:** the cone MCU **restarted at least 3× on 2026-07-12** (`18-34-09` dropout t+32.3→t+50.3 s; `18-44-56` t+12.4→t+26.7 s; `20-03-36` **down at launch**, up at t+43.9 s — each returning with `last_catch_seq=0` + `have_any_catch` cleared), and `20-03-36` (connected **155/593**, self-healing, **no bench process within 54 min**) is the partial-connectivity signature a port race CANNOT produce. The morning sessions look like the same fault held down ~9 min instead of ~20 s. **NO CODE LANDED**, deliberately: no defect reproduced in the reported path, and the interlock closing the (real, latent) co-bind hazard lives in shared transport the parallel gain-tuning workstream is actively depending on — a **coordinate-first change, not a drive-by**. Probe hardened alongside (`tools/probes/cone_bag_decode.py`): reboot-aware `taps` (a seq reset was silently bridged ⇒ ~250 phantom taps had a catch followed either reboot — so the `taps == catch_ev` 17/17 transport validation is **conditional on no post-reboot catch**, now stated), a genuine ZERO no longer collapses to "topic absent" (which had silently suppressed the *exact* catch-frames-lost case the probe exists to detect — on BOTH the statistics and decode paths), and a clean error on a missing `--root`. READ-ONLY investigation — evidence is the 17-bag decode + a 22×17 bench/session overlap check (0 pairs) + `git log --diff-filter=A` provenance + `~/.ros/log` node rosters/teardown records + live process forensics; **no test-count triple is cited because none applies to an entry that lands no code** (inventing one would be fabricating evidence). Operator confirms cone syncing works post-investigation — but note that is **consistent with an INTERMITTENT fault not currently manifesting**, and is NOT evidence the single-owner discipline cured anything. OPEN ITEMS: (a) the single-owner `flock` interlock in `TeensyLinkClient.start()` so a second binder **fails LOUDLY naming the current owner** — closing the whole **two-owners-of-one-link** class at the single point where ownership is established (needs coordination with the bench/gain-tuning workstream; **latent, never observed to fire** — fix it because a silent failure mode with no observable is a bad thing to keep, not because it is currently hurting us; interim mitigation: never run `jugglebot_launch.py` concurrently with the bench harness, pre-flight `pgrep -af bench_leg_sysid; ss -lup \| grep -E ":5005\|:5006"`); (b) `teensy_bridge_node.on_shutdown` **exceeds the launch's 5 s SIGINT→SIGTERM window in EVERY evening session** and is killed mid-profiled-stow (**exit `-15`**; its budget is ~8 s = settle + up to 6 s `DEACTIVATE`) — firmware deferred-stow is the backstop so no immediate danger, but the clean teardown is truncated every session; fixing it moves shutdown timing ⇒ needs the CLAUDE.md control-system-implications analysis, deliberately NOT done here; (c) **PROMOTED to the leading explanation** — the cone MCU restarts are unexplained if the operator did not power-cycle the cone; next steps are (1) ask the operator about ~18:35/~18:45/~20:03, (2) log a firmware **reset cause** (brown-out vs watchdog vs external) — one heartbeat field that distinguishes a power fault from a firmware hang, (3) then investigate physically. **Do not re-open the co-bind attribution without direct evidence of two live `TeensyLinkClient` processes.** | [2026-07-12-cone-sync-udp-link-contention](2026-07-12-cone-sync-udp-link-contention.md) |

Also rejected: reset on BB-heartbeat connected=false (mocap calibration independent of the BB CAN link; flickers on transient dropouts); mocap publishing success=false on startup (spurious failure event, no discriminator). | 2026-07-06 | resolved | canhub-hardening (Tier-2 residual) | **health_of() bus-off wiring — live FLTCONF joins RX staleness in the per-bus health classification**, closing the last shipped `TODO(bench)` in the bus-health path. Previously staleness-only: a bus in error-passive/bus-off with frames still flowing classified OK and accepted motion; `BusHealth::BUS_OFF` was unreachable, so the host's fatal-CAN OR-term, shutdown-stow skip, and part of the recovery-edge trigger were dead code. Fix: `BusRxHealth.flt_live` (live ESR1.FLTCONF, clamped 0/1/2) sampled per 1 kHz service tick from the SAME ESR1 read as `synced` (zero new register traffic, non-sticky — recovers with the bus); pure header-inline `classify_bus_health()` (never-seen→UNKNOWN; bus-off→BUS_OFF; passive OR stale>2s→WARN; else OK), natively pinned. WARN keys on error-PASSIVE (128) not the CAN warning level (96) — the measured 12 V-ramp REC burst (~121) sits between them, so 96 would flag every normal power-on. Strictly per-bus (operator-stipulated): a CAN1 fault cannot gate CAN3. `[canhealth]` gains `fltNow=` beside the sticky `flt=`. Bench-validated (2 operator-run checks): BB unplug/replug → `bb` fltNow active→passive→active with CAN3 untouched; CAN3 12 V cycle → WARN through the outage, clean recovery + exactly one host conservative re-read. Check 2 surfaced a **new known-benign signature** (documented, deliberately NOT hardened): the 12 V reconnection ramp punches sticky `flt=BUSOFF`/`tec=254` via a real ms-scale self-healing bus-off (gate reopens on the first valid frame while the medium is still dirty; BIT1/STUFF errors +8 TEC apiece from the 128 pin through 256; auto-recovery BOFFREC=0) — `fltNow` never shows it, no gate misbehaves, fresh-BOOT flt/tec rule unchanged. Native 16/16 (170.23 s, 2026-07-05); full suite 2054 pass / 1 xfail (651.63 s pre-commit re-run, 2026-07-06); flashed 2026-07-05 | [2026-07-06-canhub-health-of-busoff-wiring](2026-07-06-canhub-health-of-busoff-wiring.md) |
| 2026-07-05 | resolved | deactivate (operator request) | **Deactivate is a no-op when the platform is already at STOW** (hardware-validated 2026-07-05: no-op when stowed, still lowers when not). Operator ask: the legs should NOT deactivate if already stowed (all target legs IDLE with pos near stow). Previously `/deactivate` (and the activate-or-deactivate service + stow-on-shutdown) always ran the firmware DEACTIVATE — re-arming each leg to CLOSED_LOOP (a jolt), lowering to a no-op STOW, then IDLE. Guard added at `_run_deactivate` (the single enforcement point all three callers route through): `_legs_already_stowed(axes)` returns True iff every target leg is IDLE and `|pos| <= _STOW_POS_MAX_REV` (0.2 rev; STOW=0.0 in the DeactivateMonitor convention, `abs()` so it's sign-convention-safe — a bare `pos<=0.2` would wrongly skip when an active pose reads negative, leaving the platform UP). Fail-SAFE: missing telemetry / a missing diagnostic / any leg not-IDLE-or-extended / a NaN position (adversarial-review catch: `abs(NaN)>0.2` is False, so a faulted-active-leg-at-NaN would else look stowed) → False → the deactivate proceeds (only skips on a POSITIVE all-stowed confirmation; the no-op still idles the HAND for de-energise-all-axes parity). Host-only, no reflash. +3 node tests (no-op fires no leg RPC but idles hand; still-extended proceeds; predicate incl. NaN + missing-diag). `pytest tests/ -q` 2054 pass / 1 xfail (645.50 s, 2026-07-05) | [2026-07-05-deactivate-at-stow-noop-guard](2026-07-05-deactivate-at-stow-noop-guard.md) |
| 2026-07-05 | fix-landed-pending-hardware-confirm | canhub-hardening Tier-2 [18A] | **HomingResult uplink closes the homing silent-abort false-success class**. Firmware `finish()` IDLEs a leg on BOTH a successful trip AND every abort (bus-down / guard-E-STOP mid-move); only success also runs `set_absolute_position`. So a mid-drive abort was INDISTINGUISHABLE from success to the host's CLOSED_LOOP→IDLE state-cycle observer → it declared the leg homed while the encoder reference was never set (uncalibrated zero, orchestrator proceeds). Fix: uplink the firmware's real per-axis `HomingResult` (NONE/RUNNING/OK/FAILED) by reusing the Diagnostic's 3-byte pad as `homing_result` (u8) + 2-byte pad — size-preserving (36 B) but a layout change → **`PROTOCOL_VERSION` 1→2** (freeze `_EXPECTED` re-pinned + version-freeze bumped; firmware+host lockstep). Host `HomingMonitor` now TRUSTS the result (OK→DONE, FAILED→FAILED), gated on seeing HOMING_RUNNING this run (a stale prior-home result can't terminate the new one); the state-cycle success inference dropped, timeout + active_errors backstops kept. Firmware `send_diag` populates it + a `homing_result` transition force-emits a Diagnostic (delivering the RUNNING transition promptly, decoupled from the lagging ODrive axis_state, so `saw_running` arms reliably). +7 host tests incl. the false-success regression; 4 home-flow ros tests + freeze/version tests updated. Suite 2051 pass / 1 xfail (469.60 s, 2026-07-05); `pio` green (225600 text). | 2026-07-05 | resolved | canhub-hardening (marginal-CAN3 follow-up) | **canhealth `err=` now counts wire errors** — observability follow-through on the marginal-CAN3 mislabel (the 1 Hz headline field printed the raw FlexCAN_T4 ESR1-change snapshot counter, which climbs ~200/s in NORMAL sync-active operation on benign IDLE/RX/TX activity flips — the root of the multi-session "bus erroring 10-15/s" ghost hunt). Native suite 16/16 (157.35 s, 2026-07-05); flashed same day; 45 s bench capture: all buses err=0 flags=0x00 tec=0 rec=0, chg climbing only on active CAN3, zero [canerrs]/[canesr1] lines, 7/7 ODrives heartbeating. | 2026-07-04 | fix-landed-pending-hardware-confirm | canbridge-foundation (Phase-4 follow-up) | **Stow-on-shutdown ported from can_node → teensy_bridge_node** (β migration gap surfaced during the Tier-2 powered sitting). Ctrl-C of `jugglebot_launch` while un-stowed left the platform holding — `can_node.on_shutdown` always did a profiled stow (`_gently_move_to_setpoint(0.0, deactivating=True)`); the α→β migration only ported transport teardown. **NOT a Tier-2 firmware regression** (git-blame `0ad3d25^..192e6af`: the flashed changes touched stow EXECUTION, never a trigger; the stow-on-link-loss path never existed). Deliberately fixed **host-side, not firmware**: an autonomous firmware stow-on-LINK_LOST would re-open the 2026-05-19 hazard (dropping a healthy holding platform on a transient host stall); a clean Ctrl-C is a controlled host event → the stow belongs in `on_shutdown`, as in α. Fix: `_shutdown_stow()` called before RPC/link teardown (MPC already quiesced) → guarded `_run_deactivate(deactivate_axes)` (TRAP_TRAJ lower to STOW + IDLE, hand idled), guards mirroring can_node (skip on CAN3 `BUS_OFF`/no-telemetry — deferred stow covers reconnect — or a pending deferred stow); self-bounded + best-effort. New `stow_on_shutdown` ctor kwarg (default True; tests pass False via `_build_paired_node`). +5 guard tests; suite 2046 pass/1 xfail (2026-07-04). Operator workaround until deployed: `/deactivate` before Ctrl-C.
